# OrganicSEOOps Project Plan

Status: Canonical specification; D429 targets all planned local implementation/automated acceptance, with the current basic automated stage first and human/external gates separate. See the active ledger and checkpoint for delivery status.

This document preserves the complete agreed product and technical direction for OrganicSEOOps. It is the source of truth until a later, explicit decision updates it. Implementation documents may add detail, but they must not silently contradict this plan.

## 0. First Working Release — Frozen Scope (D426)

**D437 — current user instruction (2026-10-19): implementation only.** Work only on genuinely unfinished local implementation. Preserve completed archived work and skip cards whose remaining work is testing, evidence reconciliation or release validation. Do not run tests, test maintenance, builds, type/lint checks, scans, runtime validation or final acceptance until the user explicitly requests testing. Do not add tests during this phase. Record new code as implemented but untested; keep automated/manual/external acceptance pending. Existing authorization, tenant isolation, validation, human approval and external-operation limits still apply. D437 overrides earlier automatic-check requirements and test-based continuation triggers.

Accepted 2026-10-10 (D426): prioritize the basic first working local release. Updated 2026-10-11 (D429): the user now requests 100% of all already-planned agent-owned local work, excluding separately itemized human/external actions. Finish the current R05 repair and R06 automated validation first, then automatically continue existing roadmap cards in dependency order. R01–R06 still define basic-release acceptance and R07 external public readiness; an unavailable manual walkthrough stays pending but does not indefinitely block unrelated roadmap coding. This current direction supersedes D426's permanent roadmap deferral and stop-after-core boundary; it does not waive source/DB ownership, security, human approval or release evidence.

Deliver one complete journey: verified account → organization/project → scoped Search Console CSV import or configured sync → bounded crawl/performance → evidenced findings → intent/page mapping and approved action → sourced research → readable content brief. Reuse the implementation already present; repair blockers to this journey rather than rebuilding it. Preserve simple manual change logging, technical verification, honest unavailable/outcome states and existing manual local assessment where relevant.

Retain account/tenant authorization, private artifacts, safe fetching, validation, quotas, signed billing, auditability, human approval, accessible core controls and data-loss protections. Existing basic trial/billing, bounded export and disabled-by-default unresolved closure flows must stay safe and honest. This milestone does not activate public billing or self-service erasure, invent legal terms, or require a complete large-tenant legal export.

After the basic automated stage, D429 authorizes the already-specified local parts of competitor/passive-security/AI-visibility/connectors/content/lifecycle/staff/acquisition/publishing/repository/scale work, subject to each original dependency, accepted supersession and explicit capability/demand condition. This does not mandate speculative scale work without its demonstrated requirement. Preserve existing code; do not delete or expose unverified capabilities to shrink scope. Finish safe configurable/fixture-testable implementation without live credentials; missing external/manual evidence remains a separate gate.

No new implementation cards/suffixes, speculative abstractions or features outside the agreed specification. Core defects remain in their owning R01–R05 card; roadmap work reuses existing P/TOOL IDs. Collect the already-selected R06 result and finish its automated stage before broader coding; a known failing security/migration/data-loss contract still blocks its actual dependents. Reuse source-bound evidence and rerun only invalidated checks. When all planned local implementation/focused/review contracts close, select final current-source automated acceptance; neither historical passes nor the basic stage validate subsequently changed source automatically.

R06 establishes local readiness only. R07 retains actual provider/sandbox, approved content/locales/legal/support, accessibility, deployed routing/email/storage/restore and public end-to-end checks under §§34.1–34.2. External access and manual approvals remain explicit release blockers; fixtures never establish live readiness. D444 (2026-10-20) explicitly authorizes the initial Git commit and push to [amanmyrats/organicseoops](https://github.com/amanmyrats/organicseoops), with CI skipped under D437; this is source publication only. Paid calls, customer/example crawls, production migrations and deployment retain their separate authorization gates.

Historical evidence is preserved in [the archive index](IMPLEMENTATION_ARCHIVES.md). D429 uses the existing full TODO and P/TOOL contracts as the requirements inventory; archives remain byte-preserved and their old percentages/statuses never become current acceptance. Classify requirements once in [the ledger's Local completion table](IMPLEMENTATION_TASKS.md#local-completion), then inspect only the selected dependency-ready card and relevant evidence. Report per-phase/overall local implementation and automated acceptance as verified completed local requirements divided by all in-scope local requirements. List excluded human/external-only requirements with owner/reason and retain full-project totals; mixed items' manual gates stay open, unclassified/blocked local work stays unfinished, and no100% claim precedes inventory coverage and final automated acceptance.

D428 (2026-10-11 user direction) makes continuation complete the remaining local release work instead of returning repeated unchanged handoffs: collect the existing backend terminal result, apply the checked R05 export repair after its source boundary releases, repair named failures/refresh invalidated evidence, and finish the R06 walkthrough. Independent core work takes priority; when none remains, the same active session waits only for required terminal readiness under [CONTINUE.md](../CONTINUE.md#finish-the-remaining-release-work-d428). Repeated source scans, unchanged browser failures and history writes are not progress. This clarifies D375/D423's no-wait rule only at that final dependency boundary; no duplicate suite, acceptance waiver, optional expansion or timer/launcher change follows. Tool/manual gates remain unverified if unavailable.

D429 extends continuation after that basic automated stage to all existing planned local work under [CONTINUE.md](../CONTINUE.md#complete-all-planned-local-work-d429). Apply D428's bounded wait when no independent work permitted by the current stage remains. Exhaustion means no dependency-ready local work/required operation across the full selected plan, not just completion of R01–R06. Genuine conditional/manual/external blockers stay named and unfinished; the installed timer/launcher and external/Git/deployment authority are unchanged.

## 1. Product Summary

OrganicSEOOps is an authenticated, multi-project platform for improving organic traffic. A user or organization can manage several domain-based projects, connect first-party analytics, run research and audit jobs, review prioritized opportunities, and create evidence-backed content from a clear UI.

`transfertakip.com` and `airporttransferhub.com` are examples of customer sites, not preloaded or mandatory projects. A signed-in authorized user creates each organization and project through the application UI and chooses its actual domain, allowed sources and model configuration. No agent creates example project rows or contacts either example site without a separate user action in the application.

The platform starts with SEO research, technical analysis, content briefs, articles, and social content. Its job and provider architecture must allow later responsibilities such as publishing, broader social workflows, repository-aware remediation, and additional types of analysis without turning the first release into a general-purpose workflow builder.

The public SaaS product also includes a discoverable marketing site, guided onboarding, an automatic organization-level free trial after email verification and organization creation, subscription management, and understandable customer/admin workflows. A bounded AI-visibility capability will track sampled brand mentions/citations, identifiable referral traffic, and discoverability evidence alongside traditional SEO. The first internal useful release can precede commercial readiness; public trial/paid launch must satisfy the lifecycle requirements below.

## 2. Name And Positioning

The working product and repository name is `OrganicSEOOps` / `organicseoops`.

The name intentionally reflects SEO and organic growth while remaining broad enough to cover:

- Search engine optimization
- Content research and production
- Organic social media support
- Competitor intelligence
- Website performance
- Passive security observations
- Future repository-based remediation

`organicseoops.com` is the intended product domain, not yet acquired or availability-checked; it is distinct from the customer projects in Section 1. The intended host layout uses `organicseoops.com` for the single static Angular frontend (public, account and authenticated application route spaces) and `api.organicseoops.com` for Django. The public brand, product domain, and trademark availability must be checked before a public commercial launch. The internal project name does not block that later decision.

### 2.1 Public Website And Customer Journey

Provide a homepage with audience, value proposition, and a clear trial action; feature/use-case pages for available SEO, content, competitor, local, and AI-visibility capabilities; pricing with included usage and trial terms; a product preview/sample report; FAQs/help/contact; and privacy, terms, data-handling, and cancellation information appropriate to the launch markets. Use real or clearly labeled demo results, and distinguish available features from planned ones.

Public marketing pages should be crawlable, indexable where appropriate, accessible, and performant, with meaningful titles, canonicals, sitemap entries, and share metadata. Build them as Angular pages with semantic HTML templates and custom native CSS, using build-time prerendering for indexable landing/marketing content. Do not use PrimeNG or other UI/CSS frameworks for these public pages. The visual quality comes from deliberate typography, spacing, responsive composition, imagery, and restrained accessible interactions, not a generic dashboard theme. The authenticated dashboard uses CSR; runtime SSR and a production Node frontend server are not part of this deployment (Section 23.7). Login, verification/reset links, private reports, and dashboard routes must not be promoted as indexable marketing content; authorization remains the data boundary.

The target journey is public page → registration → email verification → organization/trial activation → short project onboarding → first useful findings → selected action → outcome review → paid subscription/renewal. Invitations join an existing organization without creating a fresh trial. Marketing, pricing, checkout, and in-product entitlements must describe the same offer.

### 2.2 Launch Audience And Public Page Map

The initial launch customer segment is airport-transfer businesses serving Antalya and Istanbul. Their travelers include visitors from Russia, Ukraine, the UK, Germany, the Netherlands, Belgium and Poland, as well as local customers. Those origin countries describe the transfer businesses' audiences; they do not by themselves select OrganicSEOOps' product languages, support languages, launch markets or evidenced search queries (Section 2.4). `airporttransferhub.com` is an example customer site, not the SaaS domain or a mandatory seeded validation target. This initial focus does not hard-code the transfer industry into platform capabilities or rule out later segments.

The primary launch buyer is an SEO agency with several employees managing airport-transfer clients in this segment. Lead positioning and first-use workflows with multi-client project separation, team roles, evidence review and repeatable operations. Owner-operators and in-house marketers remain supported secondary buyers/users through the same organization, project, membership and restricted-project model; do not introduce a separate agency-only hierarchy or hard-code one team shape.

The primary launch promise is to turn client-site audits and source data into evidence-backed, prioritized SEO actions that an agency team can review and execute. Technical audits, keyword opportunities and content improvement support that promise rather than competing as unrelated product messages. The first useful result should be a small defensible next-action list, not a generic score, exhaustive issue dump or claim that OrganicSEOOps automatically fixes every SEO problem.

This remains intended positioning until the findings, prioritization, action tracking and first-value cards provide representative evidence. The user's desired scope spans site structure, blog/content, rankings, keywords and other SEO issues, but public promises must follow actually supported capabilities rather than imply that all issues are already solved. Align the final copy with onboarding depth, tested first useful results, pricing and support needs.

Turn Section 2.1's page categories into a reviewed launch page/keyword map with these fields:

| Field group | Required information |
| --- | --- |
| Audience and intent | Intended visitor, question/problem, search intent, primary topic and supporting query cluster with research source/date |
| URL and locale | Proposed canonical URL, language/market, indexability, and rendering classification |
| Content and proof | Main promise, outline, title/description, available feature evidence, and approved demo/case-study material |
| Conversion | Primary action, destination, and measurable success event |
| Discovery | Parent/navigation placement, relevant internal links, sitemap inclusion, and locale alternatives where applicable |
| Ownership and readiness | Content owner/reviewer, review date, implementation/provider dependencies, and publication status |

Map a distinct useful purpose to each page instead of creating one near-duplicate page per keyword. Add integration, comparison, resource, or other landing pages only when supported capabilities and useful differentiated content justify them. Keep the map consistent with generated routes, canonicals, navigation, sitemaps, and the public/app boundary. D091 selects the intended host layout but the product domain is not acquired; actual approved route URLs and target queries remain to be evidenced, and no keyword strategy is inferred from a domain name.

The working route-level artifact is [the public page map](PUBLIC_PAGE_MAP.md). It is planning evidence, not approved copy or publication readiness; its query phrases remain qualitative hypotheses until supported research and each route's dependencies/review pass. Missing domain purchase or final bilingual approval does not block implementing and locally checking the configurable public routing/content pipeline; actual public URLs, claims, translations and publication require review and real-host evidence.

### 2.3 Proof Of Value And Marketing Content Operations

Prepare a realistic sample report that demonstrates evidence → prioritized finding → recommended action → verification/outcome context. Clearly distinguish synthetic demo data from measured results and unavailable outcomes. Case studies may use user-created projects only when sufficient evidence and publication permission exist; preserve baseline/comparison windows, actual changes, sources, and material limitations such as seasonality or concurrent work. Do not fabricate testimonials, logos, rankings, revenue gains, or causal success claims. A labeled sample report can support launch before a measured case study is available.

Under D109, authored public content initially lives in the repository's Angular public templates and reviewable route inventory, independent of customer CMS integrations. The founder edits and owns publication; a bilingual human reviews each actual English/Turkish body and metadata revision before release. A checked route/revision manifest binds approval evidence to the generated static HTML digest so a post-review edit cannot ride along with an approved build. Use draft → private local/branch preview → recorded review/approval → static build/deploy → live verification, retaining revision history and a prior compatible build for rollback. No draft/noindex preview may ship through the public release workflow; a protected hosted staging origin is a later authorized deployment check. Add an editing CMS only when non-developer publishing becomes a concrete requirement. Never publish private project exports as marketing assets.

Review public prices, trial terms, feature availability, translations, links, and proof whenever the corresponding product facts change. Rebuild and invalidate affected HTML using Sections 23.7 and 25; keep the content inventory/page map and meaningful modification dates current. Retired or renamed URLs need deliberate redirect/removal handling. Marketing publication is distinct from customer CMS publishing and must not depend on the later publishing-integration phase.

### 2.4 Launch Markets And Localization

The launch product languages are English and Turkish across public content, onboarding/application UI, critical account/billing messages and support. A project's target languages describe its SEO work and may include Russian, Ukrainian, German, Dutch, Polish or other languages without implying that the OrganicSEOOps product/support team is localized into them. The founder is the publishing owner and a bilingual human reviewer must approve English/Turkish public, app and critical-email text before release. AI or machine translation may assist a draft but never satisfies review. Unsupported product languages must not be advertised.

English public pages use unprefixed paths such as `/` and `/features`; Turkish equivalents use `/tr/` and `/tr/features`. The unprefixed English URL is also the `x-default` target. Each published locale page is self-canonical and links to its actual reciprocal equivalent; never emit Turkish alternates for untranslated placeholders. Provide a crawlable visible language switch, preserve explicit user choice, and do not force browser/IP redirects. The public path controls the current public-page language; the switch may preserve an anonymous preference without changing canonical routing. A signed-in user has an explicit English/Turkish product-language preference for app UI and critical email; missing/invalid preference falls back to English. Project SEO language never overrides product UI/email preference.

Under D103, `/preview/technical-seo-audits` and `/preview/tr/technical-seo-audits` provide paired local noindex technical-evidence drafts without customer results or published feature URLs; bounded scope/raw-rendered/canonical-link-schema/indexation distinctions remain explicit. `/preview/data-sources` and `/preview/tr/data-sources` provide paired local noindex source-readiness drafts separating local workflows/fixtures from real-provider/measurement-quality acceptance, without published `/data-sources`–`/tr/veri-kaynaklari` authority. `/preview/help` and `/preview/tr/help` provide paired local noindex HELP drafts with source/evidence/job/account/support limits and no published `/help`–`/tr/yardim` or service commitment. Human bilingual/public review remains pending. `/` and `/preview/tr/home` are paired local noindex HOME drafts with partial-readiness/no-open-offer/no-live-result limits; published `/tr/` stays excluded. Known 404 development previews use `/404` and `/preview/tr/404`; arbitrary unknown public/private URLs retain English fallback and real static-host HTTP404 semantics. The locally implemented `/sample-report` and `/preview/tr/sample-report` synthetic SAMPLE equivalents retain invented facts/unknown outcomes and explicit unreviewed draft status; published `/tr/ornek-rapor` stays excluded. The `/preview` and `/preview/tr` development-status equivalents are unreviewed noindex drafts with explicit switching, localized metadata, route-controlled EN/TR draft shell and analytics preference presentation (English noscript/private/unknown fallback) and no canonical/hreflang/sitemap authority. Critical account/security/trial/billing and known-recipient invitation mail can render recipient-specific Turkish drafts only in backend DEBUG development; production and unknown/ambiguous/unavailable recipients retain English fallback. Optional weekly-summary, daily operational and CrUX digest messages also use the current recipient preference for development-only Turkish draft subjects/bodies; source counts/dates/limitations and private links remain unchanged, production stays English, and localization never enables delivery. The account-settings API retains the saved preference separately from overall English application coverage and local email drafts. ## 3. Product Goals

OrganicSEOOps should:

- Put each managed domain in a clearly isolated project.
- Combine first-party metrics with site audits and external research.
- Explain why an opportunity is recommended and show supporting evidence.
- Connect recommendations to assigned actions, verified changes, and measured search and business outcomes.
- Use deterministic code for calculations and objective checks.
- Use AI for classification, synthesis, prioritization, research, and drafting.
- Let users select different providers and models for different jobs.
- Record model usage, latency, tokens, and estimated cost.
- Keep humans in control of approval and publication.
- Support long-running work without blocking API requests.
- Start on one EC2 instance while preserving a practical scale-out path.
- Support automatic organization-level trials and reliable subscription management before public trial/paid launch.

## 4. Explicit Non-Goals For The Initial Product

The first releases will not include:

- A visual drag-and-drop workflow or DAG editor
- Fully autonomous publication
- Automated backlink creation or backlink spam
- Automated comments, direct messages, or engagement spam
- Aggressive vulnerability scanning
- A vector database without a measured requirement
- A whole-web crawler or an internally built global backlink/search index
- Kubernetes
- A custom superadmin frontend when Django Admin covers the workflow
- User impersonation without a later security and audit design
- Hard-coded airport-transfer business behavior in the platform core
- OpenCode making and deploying production changes automatically
- Empty billing scaffolding or a custom payment system when a supported billing provider covers the commercial lifecycle
- Runtime frontend SSR or a production Node rendering server

## 5. Product Principles

### 5.1 Evidence Before Generation

Recommendations and generated content must be grounded in imported metrics, crawl results, provider data, and cited sources. The UI must make evidence visible.

### 5.2 Deterministic Analysis Before AI

Python and SQL should calculate CTR, position changes, traffic decline, duplicate metadata, broken links, Core Web Vitals thresholds, and similar objective findings. AI may explain, group, prioritize, or draft from those findings; it should not recalculate source metrics from prose.

### 5.3 Project Isolation

Every project-owned object must be scoped to its project and organization. Querysets, API permissions, storage paths, cache keys, jobs, and audit events must respect the same boundary.

### 5.4 Human Approval

AI-generated research, briefs, articles, social posts, and remediation proposals remain drafts until an authorized user approves them.

### 5.5 Provider Flexibility Without A Generic Framework

Provider and model selection will be configurable. Job types and application workflows remain explicit code. OrganicSEOOps will not initially become a generic agent orchestration product.

### 5.6 Extend By Adding Jobs And Artifacts

New responsibilities should normally be introduced as new job handlers that consume structured inputs and emit structured artifacts. Existing project, usage, audit, and approval infrastructure should be reused.

## 6. Tenancy And Ownership

The ownership hierarchy is:

```text
User
└── Organization
    ├── Memberships
    ├── Provider accounts and organization settings
    ├── Usage, trial, subscription, and entitlements
    └── Projects
        ├── Primary domain and aliases
        ├── Project memberships
        ├── Integrations
        ├── Competitors
        ├── Pages and metrics
        ├── Findings and opportunities
        ├── Jobs and artifacts
        └── Content
```

An organization is the billing, trial, and entitlement boundary. A project is the domain, data, and operational boundary.

Authorized users create organizations and projects through the application UI. The example customer domains in Section 1 are never seeded as projects (D103).

### 6.1 Project Domain Rules

- Each project has one canonical primary origin.
- A project may have explicitly configured domain aliases or relevant subdomains.
- Different domains are different projects, even when one organization owns both.
- Cross-domain duplication and content overlap may be reported.
- Project configuration may express industry, audience, market, and language, but platform logic must not assume the transfer industry.

### 6.2 Roles

Initial organization roles:

- `owner`
- `admin`
- `member`
- `viewer`

Project-specific membership can restrict access when an organization has teams working on different sites. The first owner can access every project in the organization.

Authorization must be deny-by-default. UI route guards are convenience only; Django must enforce every permission and tenancy boundary.

### 6.3 Authorization Contract

Use this baseline matrix for server-side authorization and UI affordances. Project access restrictions apply to every project action; ownership of one organization conveys no access to another.

| Action | Owner | Admin | Member | Viewer |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| View accessible project data/reports | Yes | Yes | Yes | Yes |
| Launch analysis jobs and create/edit research or drafts | Yes | Yes | Yes, within access and budget | No |
| Approve content/remediation and manage project settings/integrations | Yes | Yes, within project access | No | No |
| Create/delete projects and manage project access | Yes | Yes, within granted management scope | No | No |
| Invite/manage members and viewers | Yes | Yes, without elevating own privileges | No | No |
| Grant/revoke admin or owner status; transfer ownership | Yes | No | No | No |
| Manage subscription, payment methods, organization spend caps, or organization-wide export/deletion | Yes | No | No | No |

Authenticated project report exports follow project-read permission; creating external report shares requires owner/admin permission. Any later billing-manager or approver delegation must be explicit rather than implied by access to a screen. User self-service account/security actions apply only to that user and must preserve organization ownership invariants. Never expose decrypted credentials to any customer role.

Enforce permissions and entitlements independently: payment does not grant a role, and an authorized role cannot bypass quotas. Recheck access on sensitive actions, job execution/stage boundaries, exports, and scheduled deliveries so revocation or suspension takes effect. Audit role, credential, approval, ownership, deletion, and billing changes; prevent concurrent removal/demotion of the last active owner.

## 7. Authentication And Account Lifecycle

Registration will work as a normal self-service product flow.

```text
Register
→ verify email
→ create or name organization
→ become organization owner
→ activate eligible organization trial automatically
→ create first project
→ connect data sources
```

Required account capabilities:

- Registration with unique email address
- Email verification
- Login and logout
- Password reset
- Password change
- Organization invitations
- Invitation acceptance
- Account and organization suspension by a platform superadmin
- Session and security audit events

### 7.1 Authentication Mechanism

The Angular SPA should use secure HttpOnly cookies and CSRF protection. Authentication tokens must not be stored in browser local storage.

The frontend and API should be deployed under controlled origins, preferably subdomains of a common parent domain. Production cookie domain, SameSite, CORS, CSRF trusted origins, and HTTPS settings will be environment configuration.

Development email may use Django's console backend. Production email can use AWS SES when deployment is configured.

Platform superadmins require MFA before production access, with a tested recovery procedure. Customer MFA is a planned follow-up; social login, personal access tokens, and external API clients remain later features unless needed. Use maintained authentication components rather than custom cryptography or token formats.

The implemented browser-account foundation uses the pinned django-allauth headless browser API with Django cookie sessions. It keeps verification and login rate limiting, expiry and replay behavior in the maintained package; project code adds only safe verification-state/resend endpoints and whole-address email normalization. Verification keys travel in frontend URL fragments and are removed from browser history after reading. Production email delivery remains a separate launch dependency.

### 7.2 Custom User Model

A custom Django user model using email as the login identifier must be created before the first application migration. Replacing Django's user model later is unnecessarily disruptive.

### 7.3 Verification, Recovery, And Session Edge Cases

- Support verification resend with throttling and clear expired, used, invalid, and already-verified outcomes. Verification must not duplicate organizations or trial grants. Unverified users can recover or verify their account but cannot start expensive jobs.
- Forgot-password responses and response timing must avoid revealing whether an account exists. Use framework-supported expiring, single-use reset tokens, trusted HTTPS URL origins, and token-redacted logs/referrers. A reset request alone must not lock or modify an account.
- After a successful reset, notify the user, revoke existing sessions, and require normal login. Password changes require current authentication/re-authentication and explicit session invalidation behavior. Provide session listing, individual revocation, and logout-all.
- Email changes require recent authentication, verification of the new address, notification to the old address, and uniqueness checks. Do not silently replace the verified identifier before the new address is confirmed.
- Handle invitation expiry, revocation, duplicate acceptance, and acceptance by the wrong account. Require a verified matching identity and idempotent acceptance; invitations do not grant trial resets or privileges above the inviter's authority.
- Show suspension, session expiry, access denied, and integration reauthorization states with a clear next step. Preserve non-sensitive unsaved work where practical without leaking it to another account.

### 7.4 Ownership, Export, And Account Closure

Support organization ownership transfer to an eligible existing member with explicit acceptance and audited re-authentication. Only one transfer may be pending per organization; either participant may cancel/decline it. On acceptance the recipient becomes owner and the initiating owner becomes admin, retaining explicit access to previously accessible restricted projects. A last active owner must transfer ownership or deliberately close the organization before deleting/leaving their account. Suspending an account revokes its sessions even after reactivation; suspending an organization pauses its operations without deleting its members, pending invitations, or projects. User account deletion and organization deletion are separate operations; neither should silently delete another member's data or ownership.

Define export, closure, cancellation, and retention behavior before public launch. Organization closure must stop new/scheduled work, cancel billing through the provider as applicable, revoke integrations, and track deletion across database records, S3 artifacts, caches, and eventual backup expiry. Keep only legally/operationally required records with restricted access and disclosed retention. Explain grace periods and irreversible steps; do not claim immediate removal from every backup. Trial expiry or cancellation alone is not an immediate data-deletion command.

Trial expiry, effective paid cancellation and cancellation after failed-payment recovery provide 90 days of read-only retained results, authorized export and checkout/recovery access. After that window, delete customer content from active systems within 30 days. Deliberate owner organization closure disables access and work immediately and deletes customer content from active systems within 30 days. Customer-bearing backups expire through a maximum 35-day rotation after active deletion. Billing, fraud, security, audit and legal records may remain separately restricted for counsel-approved periods; they must not become a pretext to retain general customer content.

## 8. Platform Superadmin

Django Admin is the initial platform superadmin panel. It provides mature permissions, filtering, forms, and operational CRUD without duplicating an internal administration product in Angular.

Platform superadmins should be able to manage or inspect:

- Users, verification, activation, suspension, and staff status
- Organizations and memberships
- Organization invitations
- Projects, domains, ownership, and project status
- Integration connection status and revocation
- AI provider definitions and model catalog entries
- System model-routing defaults
- Job runs, stages, failures, retries, and cancellation state
- Token usage, provider usage, estimated cost, and latency
- Crawl and audit history
- Content state and publication audit records
- Platform feature availability
- Plans, trial eligibility/expiry, subscription state, quotas, and entitlements
- Security and operational audit events

Secrets must never be displayed decrypted in Django Admin. Passwords are never viewable. User impersonation is excluded initially because it requires explicit consent, access controls, prominent indication, and detailed auditing.

Normal organization owners will use Angular organization settings for team and project management. They will not use Django Admin.

### 8.1 Customer Organization Administration

Angular organization settings provide company details, member/invitation/project access management, subscriptions/invoices/payment links, usage and spend caps, integration reconnection/revocation, approvals/assignments, scoped audit history, exports, ownership transfer, and closure. Group billing and destructive actions clearly and apply Section 6.3 permissions. Show current plan, trial end, usage, blocked capabilities, and the next available action without exposing another organization's data.

### 8.2 Platform Support And Operational Administration

Use Django Admin for permission-scoped support workflows: account suspension/reactivation, trial/subscription investigation, audited trial extensions or entitlement corrections, failed jobs and stale connections, email failures, provider outages/cost spikes, and support requests linked to safe identifiers. Record actor, reason, before/after values, and provider references for exceptional changes. Trial extensions must preserve original eligibility history and synchronize the effective expiry/entitlements with billing. Reconcile billing through supported provider operations rather than manually marking unpaid invoices paid in the application. Staff status alone must not imply unrestricted support access; retain the initial prohibition on impersonation and decrypted-secret display.
