## 9. Trials, Subscriptions, And Entitlements

Automatic trials and billing are requirements for public trial/paid launch, not a late scale-only phase. An internal SEO prototype can precede them. Build actual organization-scoped lifecycle and usage controls in the commercial-readiness milestone after their dependencies exist; do not add empty billing scaffolding during the documentation checkpoint.

Trial and subscription entitlements may limit:

- Active projects
- Organization members
- Monthly provider spend
- AI tokens or job credits
- Crawled pages
- Scheduled jobs
- Competitor count
- Data retention
- Enabled integrations
- Available providers, models, and job types
- AI-visibility prompt sets, observation frequency, and sampled responses

Usage must be recorded from the beginning because it is operationally useful even before billing. Subscription prices, allowances, trial duration, and payment policy must be settled and recorded before activating public offers.

### 9.1 Automatic Free Trial

Once a verified user creates an eligible organization, grant its trial once, atomically and idempotently. Persist eligibility, start/end timestamps in UTC, the offer/policy version, allowances, and any audited extension. Repeated callbacks, invitations, email changes, new projects, or ownership transfers must not renew the same organization's trial. Define proportionate repeat-organization abuse controls without assuming verification alone establishes eligibility.

The trial lasts 30 days and is usage-limited, including platform-funded crawl, research, and AI costs. Each eligible organization receives it at most once after verified-user organization creation; repeated callbacks, invitations and ownership changes never reset it. No card is required for trial activation. Display the exact end date in the user's timezone, remaining days and usage, and what happens afterward. Trial expiry becomes read-only under the retained-access policy, and paid conversion requires the owner to complete explicit hosted checkout. The planned `agency-launch-v1` trial permits one active project, three organization members and 40 internal units across the trial; final advertised feature/source coverage remains gated by provider and cost evidence.

An authorized platform staff member may grant one audited seven-calendar-day support extension through the MFA-protected trial-change path. The extension preserves the original start, usage and allowances and caps the trial end at 37 days after its original start. It is available only to a matching active or expired/read-only `agency-launch-v1` trial before that capped end, never to a pending, suspended, policy-drifted, already-extended or paid organization. Require a reason, transactional locking and immutable before/after audit; a later paid-entitlement model must also reconcile this correction with provider truth.

Do not require hosted payment-method setup before trial eligibility or clock start. Payment details enter only through deliberate owner checkout for paid conversion.

When trial access ends without a paid entitlement, stop new expensive work and pause recurring schedules; queued work must recheck eligibility and running work stop at safe stage boundaries while preserving results and settling consumed usage. Provide an expired/read-only state with authorized access to retained results, billing recovery, and exports within the published retention policy. Do not silently resume all accumulated schedules or reset consumed trial credits on upgrade.

For the local trial enforcement boundary, jobs reserving one or more internal units are costly work and require an active, unexpired, exact-policy trial. Enforce the 40-unit allowance over the trial's half-open UTC window using settled actual units plus active reservations; missing historical trial state fails closed until an explicit cutover/backfill exists. Zero-unit diagnostic and ownership-verification utilities may remain available in read-only state, subject to normal authorization, suspension, reservation-integrity and internal-concurrency controls. Keep trial unavailability, exhausted trial units, internal capacity and missing reservations as distinct denial reasons across API, dispatch and costly-stage checks.

### 9.2 Paid Subscription Lifecycle

Use a supported provider's hosted checkout and customer billing portal where appropriate. OrganicSEOOps should not collect or store raw card details. Cover pending checkout/payment, active service, cancellation scheduled for period end, expired/canceled service, and failed-payment/grace/recovery states using an explicit provider-to-application mapping. Payment status, service entitlement, account suspension, and data retention are separate concerns.

Use Paddle Billing as the merchant-of-record provider, subject to seller-account approval and sandbox/live verification. Pin the current Paddle API version `1` explicitly with `Paddle-Version: 1`; recheck the official version and compatible SDK before implementation rather than following legacy Paddle Classic examples. Use USD catalog/base prices and Paddle's localized TRY price preview/checkout for Turkish buyers. The application-managed no-card trial remains separate from Paddle's subscription-trial feature. After explicit owner checkout, the selected monthly subscription renews automatically with disclosed interval, currency, tax/total and cancellation terms; Paddle's hosted customer portal is the default payment/cancellation surface, with period-end cancellation preserving paid access through the current period. A canceled subscription requires a new checkout to return.

Build checkout/portal, signed webhook/reconciliation and paid-entitlement behavior with locally simulated provider lifecycle responses before a real Paddle account exists. Supply seller credentials, webhook verification secret and catalog identifiers through MFA-protected platform-operator configuration UI (or private deployment bootstrap), and let the organization owner start hosted checkout/recovery from customer UI; no customer can enter or view Paddle seller credentials. Provider-unconfigured flows fail closed without taking payment. Sandbox/live acceptance and seller/domain/bank approval remain release gates, not prerequisites to implementing or locally checking the code.

The planned `agency-launch-v1` offer has one self-serve monthly Agency plan at a USD 149 organization base price before provider-calculated tax, with three active projects, ten organization members and 100 internal units per monthly paid entitlement period. Internal units are operational request/page work rather than currency, provider cost or customer-purchasable credits. No annual or second self-serve launch tier is selected. P3A-12 must validate affordability and actual source/feature coverage before publication or activation; current internal safety ceilings continue independently.

Owner checkout during an active trial charges immediately, ends the trial immediately and starts the first monthly paid entitlement period immediately. Disclose this before checkout confirmation; unused trial time or units do not postpone charging or extend the paid period. With one launch plan, plan-to-plan upgrades, downgrades and proration are not launch flows. Needs beyond the published limits go to contact/support without implying an unpublished custom entitlement.

Use intended USD monthly wire payout to an approved Turkish business bank account, subject to Paddle/bank approval and Turkish accounting confirmation; Paddle does not currently list TRY as a payout currency. A verified failed renewal keeps paid service for seven calendar days from provider failure, then stops costly work and pauses schedules while preserving read-only results, export and payment recovery. Verified recovery restores paid service without replaying accumulated schedules or resetting consumed usage; exhausted recovery/cancellation starts the 90-day retained-access period.

Follow Paddle's then-current refund process and mandatory law. Period-end cancellation has no automatic prorated refund. Confirmed full refunds end paid service; verified chargebacks stop costly work and leave read-only/export access while provider truth is reconciled. Support renewal, cancellation confirmation, reactivation, failed-payment notices, invoices/receipts and billing details/payment methods. If later offer versions add multiple plans, define upgrade/downgrade effective dates, proration and excess-resource handling before offering those transitions; never delete projects automatically on downgrade. Prefer provider-managed payment recovery to a custom billing engine.

### 9.3 Billing Reliability And Usage Enforcement

- Resolve billing customers/subscriptions to organization-owned records server-side. Never grant entitlement from a checkout return URL, client-supplied plan ID, or unverified webhook.
- Verify webhook signatures on the raw request body with supported libraries and replay protections; persist valid events durably before acknowledgement, deduplicate provider event IDs, and process asynchronously with retries. Handle duplicate/out-of-order delivery and reconcile against current provider state; timestamps alone are not a safe event-ordering mechanism.
- Enforce quotas in the API, scheduler, and worker before costly stages. Reserve bounded usage atomically for concurrent jobs, settle actual usage, and release unused reservations on failure/cancellation without double charging retries. Distinguish estimates, credits, provider cost, and customer billing amounts.
- Make expiry and renewal effective based on server-side state/time even if reminders or Beat execution are delayed. Keep scheduled-job billing/permission checks, reconciliation failures, and administrator adjustments auditable.
- Use a stable, documented provider integration suitable for the chosen checkout flow. Verify current API/version compatibility and test in its sandbox; a newly documented preview feature is not an automatic dependency.

## 10. Project Configuration And Onboarding

Each project should eventually collect:

- Project name
- Canonical domain and optional aliases
- Default country and target markets
- Languages and default language
- Timezone
- Industry and business description
- Products or services
- Ideal audience
- Conversion goals
- Brand names and aliases for branded/non-branded query classification
- Business locations, actual service areas, and eligible local business profiles where relevant
- Brand voice
- Seed topics
- Prohibited topics or claims
- Approved and blocked research domains
- Competitors
- Social channels
- Crawl limits and schedules
- AI routing overrides and budgets

A project can exist before ownership is verified, but active security checks, publishing connections, and other sensitive actions may require verification through a connected Search Console property, DNS record, HTML file, or another approved method. The initial local implementation supports a private owner/admin-controlled HTTP-file challenge only for the exact current canonical origin or configured alias. It stores a one-way challenge digest and verification/revocation timestamps, automatically revokes evidence displaced by project-scope changes, and requires sensitive callers to recheck current verified scope rather than treating project creation, competitor selection, or ordinary crawling as ownership proof. This point-in-time evidence remains effective until explicit revocation or removal from project scope; any later age-expiry policy must be defined by the consuming sensitive feature.

### 10.1 Progressive Onboarding And First Value

The first-use wizard asks for website, target country/language, and primary business goal, then offers analytics connection or a supported file import and a bounded first audit. Explain requested integration access and expected value. Move detailed brand voice, feeds, provider overrides, and advanced limits into optional later setup; use visible editable defaults where appropriate.

Save/resume onboarding, show verification/connection status, and allow useful work when an integration is skipped or unavailable. Offer clearly labeled synthetic demo data/sample reports isolated from live project data and billing. Show first-job progress, partial results, failures, and cost/credit limits. The first-results screen should explain a small set of prioritized next actions (aim for three when evidence supports them), not fabricate findings to fill a quota. Capture activation milestones such as first successful import/audit and first accepted action using privacy-conscious product metrics separate from customer website analytics.

## 11. Data Sources

OrganicSEOOps is not limited to Google Search Console.

### 11.1 First-Party Sources

| Source | Purpose | Initial approach |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Google Search Console | Queries, pages, clicks, impressions, CTR, position, indexing, sitemaps | CSV import first or alongside OAuth API sync |
| Google Analytics 4 | Landing pages, engagement, conversions, revenue, acquisition | OAuth and Data API |
| Bing Webmaster Tools | Bing search and indexing signals where available | Planned connector after core Google integrations |
| Yandex Webmaster | Yandex search and indexing signals where available | Planned connector after core Google integrations |
| Additional webmaster platforms | Engine-specific first-party search signals | Add connectors when supported access and project demand exist |
| Sitemap and robots.txt | URL discovery and crawl directives | Native fetch and parse |
| Project website crawl | Metadata, links, canonicals, status codes, schema, content | Native crawler job |
| CMS | Existing and published content | Provider-specific integration later |
| CRM or conversion data | Business value and lead outcomes | Optional integration later |
| Product or service catalog | Offers, terminology, availability, prices where relevant | Upload or API later |
| Social account analytics | First-party post and audience performance | Platform APIs or exports, subject to access |
| Business profiles and local listings | Business details, reviews, and supported local performance signals | Manual review/import first; authorized APIs where available and eligible |
| Authorized server/CDN access logs | Bot requests, crawl frequency, status codes, and crawl waste | Optional bounded import after core crawl and indexation diagnostics |

### 11.2 Performance Sources

| Source | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| PageSpeed Insights API | Lighthouse lab audits and improvement details |
| Chrome UX Report API | Current real-user Core Web Vitals at origin and page level |
| CrUX History API | Historical real-user performance trends |
| Optional self-hosted Lighthouse | Repeatable controlled audits when needed later |

PageSpeed and CrUX data must remain distinguishable. Lab observations and field data are not interchangeable.

### 11.3 Trends And Topic Sources

| Source | Purpose | Constraint |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Google Trends API | Search-interest trends and regional comparisons | Official API currently requires alpha access |
| Search Console query changes | Trends already reaching the project | First-party and high value |
| YouTube Data API | Recent videos and topic activity | Quota and policy limits |
| Industry RSS feeds | Reliable niche updates | Project-configured feeds |
| News APIs such as GDELT or NewsAPI | Current news and topic discovery | Licensing and quotas vary |
| Social platform APIs | Platform-specific topics and account analytics | Access is restricted and changes frequently |
| Google Ads Keyword Planner | Keyword ideas and volumes | Ads account and API access constraints |

There is no single authoritative API for all daily popular topics. Trend sources must be selectable per project and treated as signals, not automatic publishing instructions. Unofficial Google Trends scraping must not become a critical dependency.

D117 selects GDELT DOC 2.0 ArticleList RSS as the initial bounded news-metadata connector: explicit current project seed phrase/publisher origin and one/seven-day window, at most twenty provider-returned title/link/date records with GDELT attribution. Publisher bodies/images are not licensed or fetched by discovery; translingual matches and top-N news coverage do not establish target-market demand, competition, popularity or complete history. Reuse protected fixed-host requests and project-owned discovery/review jobs. Actual source readiness remains P3A-12; optional YouTube and official Trends require their own supported contracts/configuration and stay unavailable until implemented.

D118 requires a separately labeled YouTube public-search metadata flow with unchanged provider order/requested metadata, dedicated platform key/client-wide quota and bounded expiry/deletion. Current provider policies constrain search-result intermixing and API-derived metrics; do not merge YouTube results into generic topic scores, AI research, durable content or export copies. Public search requests no private-user OAuth. No media download/embed or quota-circumventing BYOK. Metadata cannot be exposed until its current-scope/policy-acceptance, retention and credential consumer are checked; actual policy/legal/bilingual/provider readiness remains release evidence.

D119 implements that lifecycle using response-only delivery instead of D118's proposed seven-day stored cache: private no-store responses, fifteen-minute component-memory expiry and explicit clearing, no returned API data in database/artifacts/exports/backups. A dedicated platform key and immutable API project share a conservative operator-set daily request ceiling across tenants; rotation does not reset usage. Existing scoped metadata-free attempt/usage records enforce commercial budgets, uncertainty without replay and current-source/actor/key delivery checks. A single ten-second request is a bounded synchronous exception to long-running Celery processing, with no automatic paging/retry.

### 11.4 Search And Competitor Sources

| Source | Purpose | Constraint |
| --- | --- | --- |
| DataForSEO (initial local adapter, D438) | Explicit Google top-ten organic observations and finite rank schedules | Paid; staff-reviewed capability/price and application estimated exposure; actual access/terms unverified |
| Public competitor crawl | Public titles, structure, schema, links, and publishing activity | Must respect limits and policies |
| Ahrefs, Semrush, or similar | Keyword research, backlink indexes, traffic estimates, and authority scores | Paid; provider-specific methods and coverage |
| OpenAI web search or another search provider | Research and cited sources | Provider cost and provider-specific behavior |

OrganicSEOOps must not scrape Google search result pages directly as a core product dependency. Use an API with suitable terms.

P11-02 local source is implemented but untested under D437 (2026-10-19): DataForSEO Google Live Advanced, one plain query/explicit location/language/device, top10/one page, desktop-Windows/mobile-Android. Staff write-only configuration uses existing provider encryption, reviewed code labels, expiring price review and customer-free conservative UTC monthly exposure that survives customer deletion and key rotation. The API has no provider-enforced per-request cost cap; estimates and unknown charges are explicit. Finite paused-by-default schedules authorize at most30 occurrences/days; provider contention defers a current slot, missed slots never cause a catch-up purchase, failures/unknown costs stop repeats. Private history/detail/cancel, exact-origin URL matches, source task/date/digest/features, tracking start/gaps and bounded reviewable domain overlap reuse current job/schedule/usage/lifecycle. This live source has no historical backfill. Migration0020 is written/unapplied; no tests, builds, runtime/migration or paid provider operation occurred. Real licensing/retention/access/pricing/spending controls and representative evidence remain operator gates. [D438](DECISIONS.md#d438-first-configurable-serp-provider-and-finite-observations), [operator notes](PROVIDER_READINESS.md#serp-local-implementation--not-provider-readiness).

### 11.5 Truth And Confidence Labels

The UI should distinguish:

- First-party measured data
- Publicly observed data
- Third-party estimates
- AI interpretation
- AI-generated draft content

Competitor traffic and search volume from estimation providers must be labeled as estimates. Authority and difficulty are provider-specific scores. Backlink records and SERP results are observations within the provider's coverage, not exhaustive truth; distinguish observed records from estimated totals. Authorized competitor analytics can supply first-party measurements but do not make unrelated provider scores or estimates measured facts.

### 11.6 Targeted Crawling And External SEO Data

OrganicSEOOps will crawl project sites and explicitly selected competitor sites, not the whole web. This supports technical audits and competitor content analysis, but crawl coverage is not the only difference from platforms such as Semrush and Ahrefs: their external datasets, historical coverage, and analysis methods also matter. Feature availability must reflect the sources actually connected; this plan does not claim equivalent coverage or accuracy.

| Capability | What targeted crawling or first-party data provides | Additional requirement |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Technical SEO audits | Status codes, metadata, canonicals, directives, internal links, and observed content | Dedicated performance sources and optional rendering where needed |
| Competitor content analysis | Public page structure, topics, freshness, and content gaps within observed coverage | SERP data for search competition; a crawl alone does not establish rankings |
| Backlink analysis | Outgoing links from crawled pages and incoming links incidentally observed within that crawl set | External backlink index for broader referring domains, anchors, and link history; disclose index coverage |
| Keyword research | Queries already exposed by connected webmaster properties and topics found on pages | Keyword providers for wider discovery, search-volume estimates, and documented difficulty scores |
| Rank tracking | Connected engines may report aggregate position for owned properties | Scheduled SERP API observations by engine, keyword, location, language, and device for tracked project and competitor ranks |
| Competitor traffic estimates | Public pages do not reveal a competitor's actual traffic | External estimation provider; actual analytics only with authorized first-party access |
| Historical comparisons | Snapshots collected since project tracking began | Provider-supplied historical data where available, with explicit coverage dates and gaps |

Search Console average position is not an exact point-in-time rank. Keyword difficulty and authority scores are provider-specific measures, not universal facts; preserve their definitions and versions where available. Do not invent traffic, keyword volume, difficulty, or a complete backlink profile from page text or AI output.

External keyword, SERP, backlink, and traffic-estimation integrations are explicit product capabilities introduced in phases, subject to provider access and budgets. Missing access or missing data must be displayed as unavailable, not as zero or a passing result. Label provider, collection time, market/device scope, coverage, and freshness alongside estimates and observations. Store dated snapshots for trends and disclose when historical backfill is unavailable.

### 11.7 Extensible Webmaster Integrations

Google Search Console is the first connector, not the permanent search-data boundary. Bing Webmaster Tools and Yandex Webmaster are planned additions; other engines can follow without rebuilding project ownership, synchronization, findings, or dashboards.

- Use a small connector boundary with explicit provider-specific sync handlers, not a generic integration framework. Reuse integration connections, import runs, sync cursors, jobs, usage, and audit infrastructure.
- A project can connect multiple engines and, where required, multiple properties from an engine. Identify each connection/property with its provider and external property identifier under the owning project; validate that it matches the configured domain scope.
- The user creates project/domain records and authorizes each source in the UI; example sites in the plan are not seeded. Search Console domain-wide properties may be selectable after a project-bound domain-scope contract is implemented and verified. Domain-wide aggregates cannot be attributed to one exact origin without a provider-supported, server-validated filter; otherwise mark that dimension unavailable. The initial domain page report uses only the first provider top-row page so pagination cannot reveal unapproved sibling-host cardinality; unreported in-scope pages remain unknown. Never require the user to create an extra URL-prefix property solely to develop the connector.
- Keep provider, property, date range, query/page dimensions, country, device, search type, and other supported dimensions on imported records where available. Normalize only comparable fields and retain source payloads or provider-specific metadata for interpretation.
- Preserve engine-specific definitions, aggregation, timezone, freshness, retention, row limits, and missing dimensions. Do not merge rows across engines or present incompatible metrics as directly comparable. Dashboards and detectors must be source-aware, with engine filters and explicit rules for any combined view.
- Declare capabilities such as query/page metrics, indexing information, sitemap operations, history, and supported import/sync methods. Do not assume every engine has Google-equivalent APIs, OAuth, or metric coverage; gate UI and jobs by actual capabilities.
- Keep authentication, ownership checks, credential revocation, pagination, incremental windows, and backfills provider-specific. Verify current official documentation and access requirements before selecting endpoints or making feature promises. Supported exports can be imported when API access is unavailable.
- Scope idempotency, checkpoints, schedules, rate limits, and failures by provider connection/property and project. Bound backfills, respect provider/account quotas and retry guidance, and expose per-connection freshness and partial failures so one engine does not block the others.
- Scale synchronization through the existing Celery queues and independently scalable workers when measured load requires it. Adding an engine should add its connector, mappings, capability configuration, and checks rather than a separate data platform.

P06-09 local implementation (2026-10-19, D437, untested): a private, manually authorized Yandex advanced query-by-URL CSV path reuses immutable project JobRun evidence, source-import usage, audit and customer lifecycle. It accepts at most100 rows/24KiB parsed evidence from a1MiB UTF-8 upload, rejects overflow without truncation and never retains raw CSV/file names. Rows preserve provider, exact configured origin, inclusive source-calendar dates, query+URL+region grain and provider ranking; timezone/coverage remain explicitly unspecified/partial. Private readback requires current reader access plus original uploader/source authority; no Google metric aggregation. Yandex OAuth authorization-code/S256-PKCE, encrypted device-token grants, exact verified host binding, write-only staff application setup and private connect/disconnect controls are also written but untested; migration0018 is unapplied. Pending/unknown credential cleanup is part of closure/deletion; a human revocation report remains distinct from provider confirmation. Bounded site-level date/device-ALL API history, refresh-token integration, independent existing connection/cursor/job/usage records, manual incremental windows/backfills, partial coverage and private history/detail/cancel UI are also written but untested; migration0019 is unapplied. This API path supports at most31 source-calendar days per request within a local90-day request bound, not guaranteed provider retention; original timestamp offsets are preserved. Contiguous cursor progress uses returned daily show/click counts; missing position averages remain unknown. Unknown refresh outcomes stay blocked until explicit human-reported remote revocation. Source/health/onboarding/export adapters keep engine semantics separate. Indexing/sitemap operations are not supported capabilities. [Configuration and official auth contracts](PROVIDER_READINESS.md#yandex-local-implementation--not-provider-readiness). [Official export contract](https://yandex.ru/support/webmaster/en/service/queries-export); implementation status belongs to [P06-09](IMPLEMENTATION_TASKS.md#p06-09--yandex-and-demand-driven-webmaster-extensions-in_progress). No automated acceptance was performed.

### 11.8 Analytics Quality And Measurement Context

Prioritization and outcome reports must expose the reliability of the underlying analytics, not just whether a connection succeeds.

- Record business conversion definitions, event/key-event mappings, values, currency, timezone, and attribution settings. Review missing or duplicate conversion events and inconsistent values using authorized evidence; an API connection alone does not verify instrumentation. Support manual verification notes where automated checks cannot establish correctness.
- Surface consent-related collection gaps, modeled data where identified, reporting thresholds, freshness, and tracking/configuration changes. Missing conversions must not be interpreted as proven zero business value.
- Separate branded, non-branded, and unclassified search queries using project-configured brand aliases and reviewable classifications. Disclose hidden or unavailable query coverage; do not imply complete query-level conversion attribution from aggregate webmaster and analytics reports.
- Keep clicks, sessions, conversions, and revenue definitions distinct. Explain attribution assumptions and source/date/dimension mismatches rather than forcing webmaster totals to reconcile with analytics totals.
- GA4 `hostName` plus landing path cannot establish the configured HTTPS origin when another scheme or port shares that hostname. Keep landing/acquisition sync unavailable until a provider-supported, source-compatible report grain can prove the exact origin; a configured web stream, property access and manual goal/event mapping are not that proof. Do not expose ambiguous historical page metrics as project-owned evidence or infer goal-specific conversions from aggregate key events.

## 12. Site Crawling

The initial crawler should prefer HTTP fetching and HTML parsing. Use browser rendering selectively for JavaScript-dependent content or requested raw-versus-rendered diagnostics, with explicit URL samples and budgets rather than rendering every page.

Crawl targets are project domains/aliases and explicitly selected competitors, with separate scoped runs. External links may be recorded without recursively crawling their destinations. Do not turn competitor analysis into unrestricted web discovery or claim that a bounded crawl represents the whole site or web.

Crawler responsibilities:

- Seed URLs from the primary domain, sitemap, and configured URLs
- Respect project boundaries and configured limits
- Capture requested URL, final URL, redirect chain, and status
- Parse title, description, headings, canonical, robots, hreflang, and links
- Parse structured data without claiming that schema is valid before validation
- Record content hash and important changes
- Identify internal and external links
- Store raw or large artifacts in S3 where appropriate
- Avoid repeatedly downloading unchanged pages when validators are available
- Rate limit per host
- Record failures clearly

Crawler security requirements:

- Block loopback, link-local, metadata-service, and private-network addresses by default
- Revalidate DNS targets to reduce rebinding risk
- Restrict schemes and ports
- Limit response size, redirects, execution time, and total pages
- Treat fetched content as untrusted
- Never execute downloaded code on the API host

### 12.1 Deeper Technical Diagnostics

- Compare raw HTML and rendered snapshots for meaningful differences in content, links, metadata, canonicals, and robots directives. Record fetch/render timing and failures; a browser snapshot does not prove how a search engine rendered the page. Browser requests must preserve crawl scope, network protections, isolation, and resource limits.
- Resolve in-scope canonical targets and check their status, redirect chains, indexability signals, and canonical conflicts. Record out-of-scope or unchecked targets as unknown rather than expanding the crawl automatically.
- Calculate internal-link depth from configured entry pages and identify weakly connected or orphan candidates against sitemap, analytics, and known-URL inventories. Report depth and orphan status relative to actual crawl coverage.
- Detect pagination gaps and crawl traps from faceted navigation and URL parameters. Preserve meaningful variants; apply project-specific parameter handling and URL budgets rather than stripping every query string or prescribing blanket canonical/noindex rules. The initial project contract uses exact case-sensitive parameter-name lists for ignored/tracking, pagination, and facet parameters plus a per-path parameterized-variant cap. Ignored names are removed only from crawl identity so configured tracking variants do not consume separate requests; pagination, facet, and unknown parameters retain their values and remain distinct within the cap. Overlap is invalid, unlisted parameters are preserved, and skipped variants remain explicit coverage evidence.
- Validate structured-data syntax and applicable type/property requirements against a recorded ruleset, including consistency with visible content. Distinguish schema validity, engine-specific rich-result eligibility, and actual search appearance; none guarantees the next.

### 12.2 Indexation And Bot Visibility

Reconcile sitemap entries, internal crawl discoveries, known analytics URLs, and available webmaster indexing/inspection reports into a per-URL evidence view. Keep discovery source, crawlability under observed conditions, indexability signals, and engine-reported indexed status separate. Preserve engine, timestamps, declared versus engine-selected canonical where available, and unknown/conflicting states. A 200 response, sitemap entry, or successful local crawl is not proof of indexing; robots blocking can also prevent observation of directives.

Optional authorized server/CDN log imports can show bot visits, last-seen requests, response codes, and disproportionate crawling of URL families. Record the log time window and coverage; verify bot identity using supported methods where feasible and label user-agent-only matches as unverified. A bot visit does not prove indexing, and absent visits in partial logs do not prove a URL was never crawled. Minimize/redact personal identifiers and sensitive URL parameters, retain private project-scoped artifacts, and define retention before accepting logs.

D122 selects an initial <=1MiB/1,000-row UTF-8 JSONL customer export with aware timestamps, exact current verified origin, relative request target, HTTP status and bounded user agent. A declared <=7-day window remains partial/customer-supplied. Parse transiently before durable job staging; discard raw logs/IPs/cookies/query/referrer/full user agents and unmatched path bytes. Only existing authorized crawl/indexation URL inventory can label retained path groups; unknown paths collapse without their text. Store private derived counts/first-last UTC observations under D098 and existing job/export/deletion controls, without independently verified bot identity or analytics-visit claims. Owner/admin source authorization and current verified origin are required; other native log formats need their own checked transformation. Intake has one marked review after focused local boundary checks; no actual customer log or bot lookup is authorized by implementation.

## 13. SEO Analysis And Opportunities

Initial deterministic opportunity detectors should include:

- Queries ranking approximately positions 4 through 20
- High-impression queries or pages with low CTR
- Pages and queries losing clicks, impressions, or position
- New queries gaining traction
- Keyword cannibalization candidates
- Missing, duplicate, short, or excessive titles and descriptions
- Non-200 internal links
- Redirect chains and loops
- Canonical conflicts
- `noindex`, robots, and sitemap conflicts
- Sitemap URLs that redirect, fail, or are non-indexable
- Indexing differences when URL Inspection data is available
- Missing or conflicting hreflang
- Missing or invalidly placed structured data candidates
- Duplicate or near-duplicate project content
- Weak internal linking and orphan candidates
- Stale content and refresh opportunities
- Pages receiving search traffic but failing to convert, when GA4 goals exist
- Pages converting well but lacking sufficient search visibility

Each opportunity should include:

- Project and affected URL or query
- Opportunity type
- Evidence and source snapshot
- Detection timestamp
- Severity or potential impact
- Confidence
- Estimated effort
- Business relevance
- Explanation
- Recommended next action
- Action status, assigned owner where applicable, implementation/change history, and outcome review

AI may group related opportunities, improve explanations, and propose action plans. It must not hide the original evidence.

### 13.1 Search Intent And Keyword-To-Page Mapping

Group related queries into reviewable intent/topic clusters using project metrics, existing pages, and available SERP evidence. Map each cluster to an existing preferred page or a proposed page, scoped by project, market, and language. Allow mixed intent, justified multiple page types, and user corrections; keyword overlap alone does not prove cannibalization.

D111 allows the reviewable mapping workflow to start from currently evidenced query-only and page-only candidates, with a **human-selected** existing/proposed page and an explicit `no joint query-page evidence` label. Do not derive a query→page relationship, cannibalization or conversion value from separate exports, and do not wait for unavailable GA4 reporting before saving honest manual intent/page decisions. Source-dependent detectors attach only after their own grain, publication-date and exact-origin contracts are proven.

D137 selects an actual Search Console API ordered query–page report as P08-01bq's bounded next source: one finalized Pacific-source day/web request over a current project-owned URL-prefix property, auto request aggregation/actual byPage response,100rows/page and at most two pages. Validate exact page-origin and query/key/metric semantics; do not join independent reports or reinterpret existing sync dimension lists. Domain-wide paired reports remain unavailable under the existing domain-page-only boundary. Positive same-query/distinct-own-page observations may support an evidenced human-review candidate only after source/job/private/native/customer-lifecycle acceptance; hidden queries, provider top-row/detail losses, missing observations and incomplete history never imply complete coverage, proven cannibalization or deletion. Publication-age and exact-origin conversion source gaps remain independent.

The selected D137 URL-prefix source is locally implemented and checked through immutable paired snapshots, explicit report/grant-revision-pinned collection, bounded private history/readback, native deliberate digest-checked inspection, response-only positive overlap previews and D098 customer export/deletion. Preview groups link exact snapshot SHA256 and row indexes; inspection creates no finding, mapping, site action or approval automatically. Latest-per-source-day/current original source and job actors/grant/property are rechecked after stored reads. Actual Google consent/provider/policy/quota/cost/hosted evidence remains unverified release work under P3A-12/P3A-13. This source-specific acceptance does not close publication-age or exact-origin conversion gaps in P08-01b.

Recommend whether to keep, refresh, merge, redirect, retire, or create content based on intent fit, existing coverage, business value, and evidence. Preserve mapping history and confidence. Research and briefs should reference the selected mapping and action rather than defaulting to a new article. Redirects, merges, and retirements remain reviewed proposals until explicitly approved and implemented.

### 13.2 Action, Change, And Outcome Tracking

The product should answer: what should be fixed next, why, and did it work? Reuse opportunities and recommendations for a small action workflow: proposed, accepted, in progress, implemented, verified, and outcome reviewed, with dismissal/reopening where needed. Approval, implementation, technical verification, and measured improvement are distinct states.

Record assignee, affected URLs/clusters, chosen action, acceptance criteria, approval, implementation/deployment date, change summary, and linked content revision or external ticket when available. Manual change logging must work before publishing or repository integrations exist. Preserve a pre-change baseline and perform a follow-up crawl or relevant data check to verify the intended fix.

Compare clicks, impressions, CTR, position, conversions, and revenue where available using explicit pre/post windows and consistent sources, dimensions, and metric definitions. Show absolute and relative changes, handling zero baselines and incomplete periods explicitly. Account for reporting delay, seasonality, year-over-year context where available, tracking changes, concurrent deployments, and known search updates. Label low-volume or insufficient-history outcomes as inconclusive. Comparisons describe observed changes; they do not prove causal uplift. Use comparison cohorts when appropriate data exists, without requiring an experimentation platform for the first release.

Under D112, immutable comparable CSV page windows and separate HTTP-first crawl observations can support honest source-limited action follow-up while GA4 exact-origin conversion/revenue is unavailable. A source row missing from a top-rows export is **unknown**, not zero. A manual implementation date and owner verification note are not evidence that a search outcome improved. Preserve distinct source timestamps/grains and link any technical verification to observed page evidence; add conversion comparisons only if a supported exact-project source is established.

### 13.3 Local SEO

Local SEO deserves early assessment for the initial transfer projects while remaining configurable for any industry. Capture real locations, service areas, business name/address/phone details, hours, services, and associated profiles. Check consistency across the site and selected listings, preserving legitimate service-area/address-display differences.

Support Google Business Profile review and authorized data access only for eligible businesses, plus other relevant local platforms when justified. Confirm current eligibility and API access before integration; start with manual records or supported imports. Do not assume each domain, airport, or service city qualifies for its own profile.

Track review themes, recency, and response opportunities with human review. Local visibility observations must retain location, query, engine/platform, device, date, and coverage, using suitable providers where required. Recommend genuinely useful airport/city/service pages grounded in actual service details and local expertise; do not mass-produce near-identical doorway pages, fabricated locations, or reviews. Direct profile edits and review responses require approval and a supported integration.

D123 splits expanded P11-05 execution: P11-05a implements separate Google Business Profile browser consent and bounded response-only profile/review reading; P11-05b retains source-permitted human review themes and exact-revision response proposals; P11-05c retains genuinely location/query/platform/device/time/coverage-specific visibility through a supported observation source. The D123/D125/D126 selected-source local rollup is now checked; real source/permission/public release evidence remains unverified. Other platforms and direct writes remain unavailable until their own supported permission/approval contract exists. GBP profile/review reads do not provide rankings or complete visibility. Initial conservative location binding also requires exact returned title/configured mapsUri against current manually reviewed business name/profile link; missing or different values require customer review, not inferred equivalence. Do not persist returned fields as assessment corrections or require address equality for legitimate hidden-address/service-area businesses.

The initial GBP reader requires explicit owner/admin business authorization, a current user-attested manual business assessment and current exact-origin ownership evidence. User-selected account/location identifiers are checked against freshly authorized account/location membership and the location's website origin; a matching domain/city alone never establishes eligibility or permission. Request only first20 accounts/first100 account locations/first50 reviews, no automatic paging/retry/schedule. Return requested metadata unchanged and in original order with attribution, no scoring/aggregation/AI or durable theme copies. No returned API content, inventories, paging tokens or response hashes in DB/Redis/artifacts/logs/topics/content/exports/backups. Dedicated app credentials/encrypted grants, customer-declared source bindings and metadata-free attempts/usage are operational records, not an API-content cache. Private no-store browser memory expires within15minutes and clears on lifecycle/scope/source changes. Bounded synchronous delivery is a feature-specific exception to long-running jobs. One marked P11-05a review follows complete focused OAuth/intake/delivery/lifecycle checks; actual app approval/quota, business eligibility/verification, provider terms/legal/bilingual and hosted privacy remain unverified release evidence under D103.

D125 selects independent owner/admin-supplied customer-owned website review/feedback as P11-05b's first permitted durable source: one<=32KiB minimized excerpt/reference/aware received date/source URL plus human theme/evidence note/reply draft, explicit storage rights and no-API-derivation. Bind the latest actual manual business assessment/configuration and current verified exact project website origin before intake/approval; do not reuse synthetic Google selection keys or copy response-only GBP metadata. No reviewer identity/contact/rating fields, automatic collection, population inference or AI are selected. Preserve private D098 export/closure/deletion/backup handling and source-stale labels. Replies use immutable draft/review/exact-revision human approval, invalidated by source/target/text edits; actual manual execution is a separate user report, never an automated provider write. Third-party/licensed sources need their own evidenced permission/retention contract and remain unavailable initially. The selected independent source/theme/reply consumers are locally checked on P11-05b; genuine source/permission/public release evidence remains unverified. D126 selects P11-05c's initial manual Google Search **organic-link** observation: one<=16KiB owner/admin customer report with own query/reference/aware date, genuine configured requested location/language/device and separately reported applied context, at most20 inspected organic links, matched verified own-website URL/human ordinal when observed, minimized note and affirmative independent storage/no-API-derived rights. Actual latest manual business/config/current verified origin and current source actor bind immutable correction history. Not seen in an inspected set is partial coverage with null ordinal; unavailable has no inspected links and cannot become rank0. Retain unknown applied context and source-stale labels. This is a human ordinal among inspected links, not universal rank, Google Maps/local-pack visibility, Search Console average position, GBP metadata or complete search coverage. No provider snippets/screenshots/competitor inventories, automated search/API import, AI, paid call, scheduling or cross-context aggregation. Other platforms/surfaces/licensed rank sources need their own contract. Private D098 history/export/closure/deletion/native consumers and selected-source local checks precede completion; real permission/terms/legal/hosted/manual-accessibility remain unverified customer/release evidence.

## 14. Performance Analysis

Performance analysis should combine:

- Lighthouse performance, accessibility, SEO, and best-practice observations
- CrUX LCP, INP, CLS, TTFB, and supported metrics
- Mobile and desktop distinctions
- Page and origin distinctions
- Current and historical field data
- Regression detection
- Affected URL templates or shared assets where inferable

The UI must explain that lab results vary and that CrUX is aggregated field data. Missing CrUX data is not a passing result; lower-traffic pages may not be eligible.

## 15. Security Analysis

Initial security observations are passive and limited to authorized project domains.

Potential checks:

- HTTPS availability and redirect behavior
- TLS certificate expiry and basic configuration observations
- Mixed content
- Security response headers
- Cookie flags visible in public responses
- Publicly exposed framework or server information
- Obviously exposed administrative paths as observations, not exploitation
- Dependency or code scanning only after an authorized repository is connected

Active scanning, credential attacks, exploit attempts, and broad port scanning are excluded. Domain verification and explicit authorization are required before any later active security capability. Passive-security jobs must bind the exact project, current origin, verification evidence, actor, and named allowed observations, then recheck them before requests and persistence.

Security findings must avoid overstating severity. Configuration observations are not proof of exploitability.

None is unavailable, not zero, and existing collapsed headers/crawl history cannot establish complete security proof. This unregistered parser grants no verified-origin/network/job/finding authority or effectiveness/score/exploitability claim. A subsequent D082-bound explicitly requested exact-origin passive collector/minimized genuine evidence and the full combined marked P12-02 review precede exposure; TLS/redirect/mixed-content/full parent requirements remain unfinished. See D230/cards.

A scheduled topic-discovery workflow may run daily or on a project-defined schedule:

```text
Collect first-party query changes
→ collect configured trends, news, feeds, videos, and search results
→ normalize topics
→ remove duplicates and irrelevant candidates
→ enrich with project and competitor context
→ score opportunities
→ show evidence and sources
→ let a user request research, a brief, or content
```

Suggested scoring dimensions:

- Relevance to products and audience
- Business or conversion value
- Existing project authority and ranking potential
- Search interest and recent growth
- Competition
- Freshness and time sensitivity
- Strength and diversity of available sources
- Existing project coverage
- Risk of duplication or cannibalization
- Geographic and language fit

Trending topics and evergreen opportunities should be separate views. Popularity alone must never be treated as relevance.

## 17. Competitor Intelligence

Users can add known competitors manually. OrganicSEOOps may suggest additional competitors based on recurring SERP overlap, but users approve additions.

Reliable comparisons may include:

- Shared and missing topics
- SERP overlap
- Public titles, descriptions, headings, schema, and page types
- Public internal-link structures
- Publishing cadence and content freshness
- Publicly measurable performance data
- Result features and content formats

Paid providers may add estimated keyword, backlink, traffic, and authority data. Estimates must be labeled and provider attribution preserved.

The expanded integration phase should include keyword discovery and volume/difficulty data, scheduled rank tracking, backlink/referring-domain analysis, and optional competitor traffic estimates through suitable providers. Rank observations must retain engine, keyword, location, language, device, timestamp, and matched URL. Preserve dated competitor and provider snapshots for historical comparisons, including coverage gaps and provider-specific definitions, as described in Section 11.6.

Competitor crawling must use host-level rate limits and should not attempt authentication, bypass access controls, or ignore applicable restrictions.
