### 17.2 AI Answer Visibility And Discoverability

Add a bounded product capability answering whether the project brand/site appears in sampled AI answers, which competitors and sources appear, and what identifiable traffic arrives afterward. It complements search visibility; it does not inspect private customer conversations or claim knowledge of all AI chats. Reuse project-scoped jobs, provider capabilities, budgets, citations, artifacts, and snapshots.

#### 17.2.1 Sampled Answer Monitoring

D127 adds a separate opt-in `visibility-repeat-schedule-v1` contract over immutable D120 prompt policies: paused-first owner/admin create/enable/data-use acknowledgment, frozen current prompt/model/account/key revision,1440–10080-minute cadence,1–30 queued occurrences/fixed1–30-day creation-relative lifetime and explicit total estimated USD<=100 covering per-request ceiling times all occurrences. Canceled slots are not refunded; pause/resume does not renew budget/deadline. One live schedule per project/set/key across revisions, shared manual>=24h cooldown, missed-slot collapse and once-only usage apply. Recheck current creator owner/admin/source/selection/commercial/lifetime at enable/due/dispatch/pre-request/completion; pause on loss/expiry/exhaustion/partial/failure/cancel/unknown, no retry/fallback/unknown replay. Repeat context stays separate in history/rates; existing D098 lifecycle/no-tools/consumer/citation/location limits remain. Complete P11A-02s fixtures/private/native consumers precede availability; actual provider/terms/hosted evidence remains unverified.

D120 starts the local methodology with owner/admin-reviewed immutable prompt sets for **native OpenAI Responses API text-only** sampling: five bounded human prompts/current intent references, separate neutral/branded groups and explicit human coverage rationale. User-selected model/account/cost at collection, unchanged prompts, no tools/retrieval/citation measurement or actual device/geolocation control; requested market/language remain human context. One selected prompt/request/internal unit per future job, manual repeat at least24hours apart, no automatic schedule/fallback/retry. `store:false` does not establish provider ZDR; prompts/evidence follow private customer export/deletion/backup lifecycle and actual provider terms remain release evidence. P11A-01 records methodology; P11A-02 implements collection/cooldown/usage. API samples never represent consumer ChatGPT sessions.

- Maintain reviewable, versioned prompt sets based on genuine customer intents, scoped by project, market, and language. Separate neutral discovery prompts from explicitly branded prompts so leading questions do not inflate apparent visibility.
- Collect through supported official APIs, appropriately licensed observation providers, or clearly labeled manual observations. Verify access/terms before promising a platform. Do not automate consumer accounts or bypass access controls to simulate coverage.
- Preserve exact prompt/version, provider/model, actual collection surface (API versus consumer observation), timestamp, requested/applied location and language context, search/retrieval mode, available settings, answer evidence, citations/links, collection status, and usage cost. Unsupported location/device controls must remain unknown rather than implied.
- Detect brand mentions, domain citations, competitor mentions, cited pages/third-party sources, and potentially incorrect business facts. Preserve evidence spans and source URLs; resolve aliases carefully and make uncertain classifications correctable. Validate link syntax/schemes before display/fetch, and treat answers/citations as untrusted. Compare factual claims to approved business facts and flag uncertainty rather than treating the answer itself as truth.
- Count metrics deterministically from evaluated observations: mention rate is answers mentioning the brand divided by valid evaluated answers; citation rate is answers citing a project domain divided by valid evaluated citation-capable answers. Count each answer once per metric, expose numerators/denominators and prompt mix, and report failed/unsupported/unevaluated samples separately. If there are no eligible samples, show unavailable rather than zero. Any competitor share metric must state its denominator and stay within the observed sample.
- Schedule bounded repeated samples to show variability and dated history. D120's initial collector remains manual-only; P11A-02s implements D127 opt-in bounded native repeats and retains additional licensed/manual surfaces behind their own reviewed methodology/access/budget policy. Compare like-for-like prompt sets, surfaces, modes, and markets; annotate model/provider or methodology changes. API answers are not equivalent to consumer ChatGPT or another application's personalized answers. These are sampled observations, not global AI market share, guaranteed rankings, or proof of future inclusion.

P11A-02sa locally checks the complete D127 native schedule/private/native/lifecycle workflow; P11A-02sb now locally checks D128's independent human report/current-authority/immutable evaluation/private native/separate denominator/export/closure/deletion workflow. The selected-source P11A-02s rollup is locally complete. Other licensed/named consumer sources require their own supported contracts; local implementation establishes neither actual consumer observations nor licensing/provider/public readiness.

D128 selects P11A-02sb's initial independently permitted customer-owned manual answer report (`visibility-human-answer-v1`/`customer_reported_answer`): exact original human prompt/version/current intent and neutral/branded group, opaque reference/source label/aware time/unchanged answer, affirmative independent use/storage/no-response-only-copy/no-personal-payload declarations under D098. No named consumer service is licensed or verified by this contract; source/model/retrieval/applied context are customer-reported or unknown, distinct from configured requested market/language. Scalar intake<=24KiB/prompt500chars/answer4000chars; immutable corrections and exact-span full-answer evaluation bind current owner/admin/project/config/intent/verified-origin authority, without fake API jobs/accounts/usage. Separate latest-report-once comparable denominators exclude partial/unavailable/unevaluated/uncertain/truncated/stale reports; citations/referrals/external cost remain null, not inferred. Selected private native/history/export/closure/deletion acceptance is locally checked; actual terms/privacy/legal/hosted/manual-accessibility remains unverified release evidence.

#### 17.2.2 Identifiable AI Referral Traffic

Use authorized analytics with versioned source/referrer/campaign classification to report identifiable AI-service visits, landing pages, engagement, conversions, and revenue where available. Keep this view separate from sampled answer metrics. Missing or stripped referrers, copied links, self-reported campaign tags, attribution settings, and consent gaps limit coverage; do not label direct traffic as AI traffic or infer a visit from a mention. Apply the analytics-quality rules in Section 11.8 and show provenance and attribution limits.

D121 separates source-independent history/discoverability (`P11A-04a`) from the retained analytics/referral requirement (`P11A-04r`). Full P11A-04 remains unfinished until both pass. P11A-04r requires P06-05's independently verifiable exact-origin reporting grain; null/unavailable referral values neither satisfy it nor block checking the supported initial API journey in P11A-05. No hostname-only attribution or synthetic visit inference is permitted.

#### 17.2.3 Discoverability And Improvement Guidance

Assess relevant crawler access, robots directives, accessible useful content, accurate entity/business details, genuinely helpful answers, truthful structured data, and credible external mentions using existing crawl/content/authority workflows. Keep search/retrieval crawlers, training crawlers, and user-triggered fetch agents distinct according to each provider's current official guidance. For example, OpenAI documents separate OAI-SearchBot and GPTBot controls; ChatGPT-User is a user-action agent, not proof of search indexing. Bot access or a bot visit alone does not prove citation, model training membership, or visibility.

Recommendations remain evidence-backed proposals requiring approval. Do not market crawler access, schema, content formatting, or an experimental file convention as a guaranteed route into AI answers. The first AI-visibility release supports a small declared platform/prompt scope, observed mentions/citations and competitors, history, and available referral data; expand only with supported access, demonstrated value, and budgets.

The initial D121 consumer reuses stored technical findings, source-linked content revisions and human factual concerns with existing opportunity review and separate action approval. Missing approved business facts, external-mention sources, verified bot logs or agent-specific robots rules remain explicitly unavailable. Current generic crawl discovery/status/meta fields do not establish OAI-SearchBot/GPTBot/ChatGPT-User access. Future raw robots capture must run through the existing explicit scoped safe application job/private artifact/lifecycle controls and leave older runs unavailable; do not fetch customer sites during agent implementation checks.

## 18. Content System

The content model must not be limited to blog posts.

Initial and future content types may include:

- Blog article
- Existing article refresh
- Landing page
- Service page
- Social post
- Social post series
- Newsletter
- Product or service description
- Custom content

Suggested content lifecycle:

```text
idea
→ research
→ brief
→ draft
→ review
→ approved
→ published
→ archived
```

Content records should preserve:

- Project
- Content type
- Target audience and market
- Primary and supporting topics
- Search intent
- Source research and citations
- Brief
- Revisions
- Author or generating job
- Approval state and approver
- Publication destination and URL
- Publication and update timestamps
- Performance snapshots before and after publication

### 18.1 Research And Briefs

A research job should collect sources before synthesis. The resulting brief may include:

- Target query and intent
- Audience and desired action
- Evidence and cited sources
- Competitor observations
- Content gaps
- Recommended angle
- Title options
- Meta-description suggestion
- Heading outline
- Questions to answer
- Internal-link suggestions
- Relevant project pages
- Claims requiring human verification
- Suggested structured data only when it truthfully applies

### 18.2 Article Generation

The AI can draft content automatically after research, but must not invent statistics, testimonials, credentials, prices, availability, or customer stories. Unsupported claims must be omitted or marked for verification.

Generated text remains a draft. The editor should show source material, brief, draft, and revisions clearly.

### 18.3 Social Repurposing

Approved research or content can be repurposed into platform-specific posts. OrganicSEOOps should create native variants rather than posting identical text everywhere.

Direct scheduling and publishing are later integrations because platform permissions and APIs differ. Manual export is sufficient for the first content release.

### 18.4 Initial Transfer-Sector Use Cases

The first projects may benefit from airport, city, route, destination, service, FAQ, local-intent, and comparison content. Those are project-level topics, not platform-level assumptions.

OrganicSEOOps should help detect duplicate or overlapping content across user-created projects where authorized, rather than automatically reusing the same article across domains. The two example sites require no special records or checks.

### 18.5 Content Quality And Lifecycle Management

Maintain a content inventory linked to page/intent mappings, business facts, sources, revisions, review dates, and responsible reviewers. Flag outdated claims, thin or overlapping coverage, obsolete service details, and refresh candidates using evidence rather than word count alone. Require original business expertise, useful first-hand details, and verified service information where relevant; citations alone do not make a draft distinctive or useful.

Offer keep, refresh, consolidate, redirect, and retire recommendations with a rationale and supporting performance, link, conversion, and intent evidence. Low traffic alone is not a deletion criterion. Preserve useful history and require review of replacement destinations and user impact before mergers, redirects, or removals. Archiving a content record does not automatically remove or deindex a live page. Feed approved changes and subsequent observations into the action/outcome workflow in Section 13.2.

D136 implements P10-05 from actual latest content/native text and private brief/current action-intent/research evidence, with exact revision/text digest/original source dates/coverage/current actor and assignee checks after private reads. P08-04a's source-qualified action/outcome work is the local prerequisite; optional P08-04b conversion attribution remains unavailable. Human expertise/service/fact-review evidence and exact-text judgments must be explicitly recorded rather than inferred from citations, configured business details, publication reports, age, traffic or word count. Existing manual local assessment is user-attested; reported publication never proves the current live page. Separate reviewed destination/user-impact proposals and existing site approval/implementation/outcomes follow the source resolver; cross-project comparison requires each project's authority, with no automatic draft copying. The private/native/history/customer-lifecycle and independently authorized saved-text comparison now pass local checks; exact body equality never proves live-page duplication, quality or service facts. Real source/permission/legal/hosted/manual-accessibility evidence remains unverified separately.

## 19. Job Architecture

Long-running work must not keep an HTTP request open. Django creates a durable application job record, Celery executes work, and Angular polls job status.

```text
Angular requests job
→ Django validates permission and budget
→ JobRun is stored in PostgreSQL
→ Celery task is queued through Redis
→ stages update JobRun and JobStep records
→ artifacts are stored in PostgreSQL or S3
→ Angular polls and displays progress
→ final output remains reviewable and auditable
```

PostgreSQL is the authoritative source for user-visible job state. Redis is transport and short-lived coordination, not the only record of job progress.

### 19.1 Initial Job Catalog

- `sync_search_console`
- `import_search_console_csv`
- `sync_ga4`
- `crawl_site`
- `audit_technical_seo`
- `audit_performance`
- `audit_security`
- `discover_topics`
- `research_topic`
- `analyze_competitors`
- `create_content_brief`
- `generate_content`
- `repurpose_content`

Additional job types can be added in code. A visual workflow builder is not required.

As their milestones ship, add explicit handlers for bounded AI-visibility sampling, customer report delivery, billing-event reconciliation, and retention/deletion work. Reuse existing job state, usage, and evidence infrastructure; scheduled jobs must recheck permissions and entitlements before execution.

### 19.2 Job States

At minimum:

- `queued`
- `running`
- `waiting_provider`
- `succeeded`
- `failed`
- `cancelled`
- `partially_succeeded` when a workflow can return useful partial results

Jobs may expose user-friendly stages such as collecting, extracting, analyzing, drafting, and finalizing.

### 19.3 Reliability Requirements

- Idempotency for synchronization and retryable jobs
- Retries only for transient failures
- Soft and hard timeouts
- Cancellation state checked between stages
- Per-provider and per-project concurrency limits
- Provider rate-limit handling
- Cost and token limits before expensive stages
- Input and configuration snapshots for reproducibility
- Prompt and output schema versions
- Actual provider response identifiers where permitted
- Clear partial-failure reporting

### 19.4 Celery Queues

Planned queues and workers:

- `default`: imports, ordinary processing, and coordination
- `ai`: provider requests and AI processing
- `crawl`: project and competitor crawling
- `audit`: PageSpeed and passive security audits

One backend image can run each process with a different command. They remain separate containers and can later scale independently.

Celery Beat runs as one separate scheduler container. Duplicate schedulers must not run concurrently.

## 20. AI Provider And Model Architecture

Users must be able to configure different models for different jobs. OrganicSEOOps should use a small provider adapter boundary while preserving provider-specific capabilities.

### 20.1 Configuration Levels

```text
System default
→ organization or project override where supported
→ individual job override
```

The exact organization-level override can be introduced when multiple teams need shared credentials. Project and individual-job selection are required product concepts.

### 20.2 Core AI Records

- Provider definition
- Encrypted provider account or credential reference
- Model catalog entry
- Model capabilities
- Task or job routing policy
- Fallback order
- Budget and token limits
- AI request and usage record

### 20.3 Model Capabilities

Capabilities may include:

- Text generation
- Structured output
- Reasoning
- Tool or function calling
- Web search
- Image understanding
- Embeddings
- Batch execution
- Background execution
- Supported context and output limits

Provider-specific options should not be erased just to create a false universal API. Adapters normalize common operations while job handlers can require capabilities.

### 20.4 Initial Provider Strategy

Start with:

- A native OpenAI adapter
- An OpenAI-compatible endpoint adapter when implementation begins

Platform-funded access remains available, but D103 also accepts scoped customer-entered BYOK for supported AI/performance providers. The platform operator configures platform-funded keys through MFA-protected staff UI; organization owner/admin configures an organization-owned key through a private write-only UI where that provider supports it. Persist encrypted secret material or private secret-store references with explicit ownership, rotation/revocation, least-privilege worker resolution and audit without ever returning a saved value. Bootstrap encryption material and AWS IAM remain infrastructure configuration, not customer-editable fields. Projects and users select supported models through the application UI according to effective credential ownership, provider capabilities and budgets; no developer-selected model ID or personal credential is a prerequisite to implement adapters/routing. Operator-supplied private environment configuration may still seed a deployment, but is not the only product configuration path. D083's earlier BYOK deferral is superseded; provider terms, actual access and costs remain release-readiness evidence, not a coding gate.

The initial D084 adapter uses the Responses protocol directly for bounded text, strict structured-object and capability-gated web-search requests. Native OpenAI uses its fixed official endpoint; a compatible provider must explicitly evidence the corresponding capabilities before selection. Both paths use one DNS-to-body deadline, public-address resolution with pinned TLS, no redirects or automatic retries, bounded bodies, categorical redacted errors and separately sanitized request/response identifiers. Structured schemas use a deliberately small fully validated strict subset rather than silently accepting unsupported JSON Schema behavior. Implement and test selection, routing, capability/fallback and bounded usage locally without a real account; real access, model behavior, costs and terms remain release-unverified until an authorized representative request succeeds.

Add native Gemini, Anthropic, or other adapters when a concrete requirement justifies their provider-specific behavior.

Model identifiers must not be hard-coded across job logic. The UI should allow enabled models, capabilities, task defaults, fallbacks, timeouts, and maximum cost to be configured.

### 20.5 Model Selection Guidance

| Work | Expected model class |
| --- | --- |
| Query classification | Cheap or small model |
| Metadata variants | Cheap or small model |
| Extraction | Deterministic parser first, cheap model when needed |
| Opportunity explanation | Cheap or mid-tier model |
| Content brief | Mid-tier quality model |
| Competitor synthesis | Reasoning-capable model |
| Final article draft | Strong writing model |
| Deep multi-source research | Strong reasoning model with search |

Free hosted models are opt-in only. Their rate limits, reliability, retention, and training policies may be unsuitable for customer or confidential data. Cheap models and deterministic processing are preferred over selecting a model solely because it has a free tier.

Local Ollama or vLLM is deferred until dedicated GPU capacity and recurring volume justify operation. Running large models on the same general-purpose EC2 instance as the application is not an initial plan.

### 20.6 OpenAI-Specific Options

Where appropriate, the OpenAI integration may use:

- Responses API structured outputs
- Web search with visible, clickable citations
- Background mode for provider tasks that take several minutes
- Batch API for non-urgent bulk processing and lower cost

Celery and OrganicSEOOps JobRun records remain the application orchestration source even when a provider has its own background mode. Provider jobs can enter `waiting_provider` and be polled without holding an API request open.

### 20.7 Usage And Cost

Every AI request should record, when available:

- Organization and project
- Job and job step
- Provider and model
- Prompt or operation version
- Input and output token usage
- Search or tool usage
- Duration
- Provider request identifier
- Estimated or reported cost
- Final status and error category

## 21. Core Data Model

Names may change during implementation, but the responsibilities must remain represented.

### 21.1 Accounts And Tenancy

- `User`
- `Organization`
- `OrganizationMembership`
- `Invitation`
- `Project`
- `ProjectMembership`
- `DomainAlias`

### 21.2 Integrations And Credentials

- `IntegrationConnection`
- `EncryptedCredential` or external secret reference
- `ImportRun`
- `SyncCursor`

Credential ownership is explicit: MFA-protected platform operator for app/seller/platform-funded keys, scoped organization owner/admin for supported BYOK, and OAuth browser consent for the user's project grant. Save entered secrets only through a write-only protected endpoint with encrypted storage or private secret-store reference, rotation and revocation; never expose saved plaintext in Admin/API/logs/task arguments. Do not mistake a user's project-domain selection for proof of source access.

### 21.3 Website And Analytics

- `Page`
- `CrawlRun`
- `CrawlPageSnapshot`
- `SearchMetricSnapshot`
- `SearchQueryMetric`
- `AnalyticsMetricSnapshot`
- `PerformanceSnapshot`

Large raw responses and files should normally live in S3 with metadata and ownership in PostgreSQL.

Search metrics must retain their integration connection, provider, external property, and supported dimensions (Section 11.7). Uniqueness and synchronization keys must include source identity and observation grain so equal dates, queries, or URLs from different engines cannot overwrite each other. Additional external keyword, rank, backlink, and traffic-estimate snapshots should be modeled only when their integrations are implemented, preserving provenance and coverage (Section 11.6).

### 21.4 Insights

- `Finding`
- `Opportunity`
- `Recommendation`
- Evidence or source links associated with findings

Findings describe observed conditions. Opportunities describe prioritized potential actions. Recommendations may be AI-assisted explanations or plans built from findings.

As these workflows ship, persist reviewable query/intent-to-page mappings, opportunity assignment and state history, change records, verification evidence, and baseline/follow-up outcome snapshots. Reuse existing page, finding, opportunity, content, and artifact relationships. Local business/profile records, authority prospects, analytics-quality notes, and optional log summaries remain project-scoped and are added with their owning features rather than as empty scaffolding.

### 21.5 Jobs And Artifacts

- `JobRun`
- `JobStep`
- `Artifact`
- `SourceCitation`
- `JobSchedule`

### 21.6 AI

- `AIProvider`
- `AIProviderAccount`
- `AIModel`
- `AIRoutingPolicy`
- `AIUsageRecord`

### 21.7 Competitors

- `Competitor`
- `CompetitorSnapshot`
- `SerpSnapshot`
- `TopicGap`

### 21.8 Content

- `ContentItem`
- `ContentRevision`
- `ContentSource`
- `Publication`

### 21.9 Operations

- `AuditEvent`
- `Notification`
- Notification preferences/delivery records, report exports/shares, and support references as those workflows ship

### 21.10 Commercial Lifecycle And AI Visibility

The commercial-readiness milestone introduces organization-owned trial grants/eligibility history, plan/entitlement snapshots, provider customer/subscription references, billing-event receipts, and usage reservations/settlement. Enforce unique trial grants and provider-event identities in storage, not only in UI logic. Provider invoices/payment details should be referenced rather than duplicating a payment ledger or storing card data unnecessarily.

AI-visibility work introduces project-owned versioned prompt sets and observations linked to jobs, provider usage, answers/citations, entity matches, and method/coverage metadata (Section 17.2). Implement records with their actual workflows; no speculative tables are needed at the documentation checkpoint.

## 22. Backend Architecture

The backend uses Django 6.1 (D041). Select its latest stable patch and current stable compatible versions of the remaining backend dependencies at scaffold time, verify official compatibility documentation, and record exact pins in `docs/DECISIONS.md`:

Initial scaffold pins and compatibility evidence are recorded in D049; actual installation/runtime acceptance is tracked by the implementation tasks and checkpoint. D051 permitted isolated non-Docker development during download failures; D053 resumes local Docker verification. Exact-target/container acceptance requires evidence in P01-04/P01-05.

D052 selects Gunicorn for the WSGI API and WhiteNoise for collected Django Admin static assets in the shared backend image, with nginx as the separate local proxy. This does not change the independent static Angular deployment or private artifact-storage boundary.

D086 supersedes D054's temporary local Redis 8.10.1 exception: the official 8.10.2 Alpine image is now pinned and verified locally with persistent broker state and the unchanged vulnerability gate. The proxy uses the same nginx release on the smaller official Alpine-slim image. D417 extends that pinned proxy base with the exact fixed PCRE2 package and a separate immutable ECR app-host release/rollback digest. Production deployment, live Redis authentication and legacy data transition require separate verification.

D056 keeps the selected runtime/framework versions while using pinned Alpine bases and build-only installer tooling to remediate inherited image findings. PostgreSQL runs nonroot and uses a new volume with builtin C.UTF-8. Existing Bookworm data requires verified logical dump/restore; the user's running stack remains unchanged until a separately authorized transition. The vulnerability gate is not weakened.

- Python
- Django 6.1
- Django REST Framework
- PostgreSQL
- Redis
- Celery
- S3-compatible object storage through AWS S3

Backend responsibilities:

- Authentication and authorization
- Organization and project isolation
- REST API
- Django Admin
- Integration OAuth callbacks and synchronization
- Job creation, validation, status, and cancellation
- Provider adapters
- Deterministic analyzers
- Content and artifact persistence
- Audit events

DRF ViewSets and routers are preferred for ordinary resources. Custom API views are appropriate for OAuth callbacks, job actions, uploads, and other command-style endpoints.

No GraphQL requirement exists.

## 23. Frontend Architecture And UI

The frontend will use Angular 22 (D041). At scaffold time, select the latest stable compatible release within Angular 22 with a matching Angular CLI, verify Node, TypeScript, and UI-library compatibility against official documentation, and record exact pins in `docs/DECISIONS.md`.

The initial frontend scaffold uses D049's exact application pins; D050 permits a compatible local Node/npm runtime while retaining the exact reproducible target for CI. Actual build and test evidence is maintained in the implementation checkpoint.

Use build-time-prerendered Angular public marketing pages and a client-rendered authenticated Angular dashboard. Deploy both as static frontend artifacts through S3/CloudFront. Node is a development/build dependency, not a production frontend service; no route should depend on request-time SSR.

PrimeNG is the default candidate for authenticated application controls, tables, dialogs, filters, and forms. The final package choice will be confirmed during scaffolding against the current Angular version.

Public landing/marketing pages use semantic HTML templates and custom native CSS only, without PrimeNG or another UI/CSS framework. Reusable Angular components and lightweight TypeScript for necessary interactions are allowed; pure HTML/CSS styling does not require abandoning Angular. Lazy-load authenticated UI dependencies and scope their themes so dashboard libraries/styles are not pulled into the public landing experience.

### 23.1 Main Screens

- Registration
- Email verification result
- Verification pending/resend and invitation acceptance/expiry
- Login
- Password reset
- Account/profile security, verified email change, sessions, and closure
- Organization onboarding
- Project onboarding
- Organization and project selector
- Project dashboard
- Integrations
- Imports and synchronization history
- SEO opportunities
- Technical SEO audit
- Performance audit
- Security observations
- Trends and topic ideas
- Competitors
- AI visibility: prompt sets, sampled answers/citations, competitors, history, and identifiable referrals
- Research jobs
- Content studio
- Content revisions and approvals
- AI providers and model routing
- Team and invitations
- Usage and cost
- Organization settings
- Project settings
- Trial status, plan selection/checkout, invoices/billing portal, and payment/expiry recovery
- Notifications/preferences, reports/exports, help, and support requests

The active organization and project should always be clear in the shell to reduce accidental cross-project work.

### 23.2 Opportunity UI

Opportunity lists should be sortable and filterable by type, impact, confidence, effort, status, source, and date. A detail view should show:

- Why it was detected
- Supporting rows, pages, metrics, or source links
- Relevant history
- Recommended action
- Job actions such as research or brief generation
- Result artifacts
- Intent cluster and mapped existing/proposed page
- Assignee, action state, approvals, change dates, and technical verification
- Baseline/follow-up metrics, analytics-quality limitations, and outcome review

Use focused views within the existing dashboard, audit, opportunities, integrations, and content screens for indexation reconciliation, local SEO, authority prospects, and content lifecycle review as their features ship.

### 23.3 Content Studio

The content view should separate research, brief, draft, social variants, sources, and revisions. Citations must remain clickable and visible when web-sourced information is displayed.

### 23.4 Job UI

Angular will initially poll a job status endpoint every few seconds while a job is active. WebSockets are unnecessary until polling becomes a measured problem.

### 23.5 UX And Accessibility Acceptance

Specify screen flows and reusable design tokens/components before implementing the main customer journeys. Use consistent typography, spacing, colors, terminology, forms, tables, and action placement. Public pages use semantic HTML/native CSS and small reusable custom components; authenticated screens may use the selected component library and native controls. The dashboard must answer how the site is doing, what matters, and what to do next, showing search/conversion trends, data freshness, priority actions, recent changes, and verification/outcome evidence.

P01-07's implementation-ready account/app flow, state, component and test conventions are recorded in [the account and application experience foundation](APP_EXPERIENCE.md). It does not replace P3A-09's later public wireframes or select unresolved commercial/positioning terms.

- Use plain-language summaries with expandable technical evidence, glossary/help text, visible active organization/project, and consistent date ranges, units, timezone, and currency. Never collapse unrelated SEO and AI metrics into an unexplained overall score.
- Define loading, empty/new-project, demo, not connected, access denied, trial expired, quota reached, stale/partial data, failed job, and insufficient-history states. Distinguish not checked, no issue found, and issue detected; missing data is not success.
- Explain job progress, partial results, retry/cancel behavior, and estimated credits/cost before expensive actions. Preserve form errors and recoverable input, avoid duplicate submissions, and show clear completion confirmations. Keep upgrade prompts understandable without obstructing permitted recovery/export actions.
- Target WCAG 2.2 AA across public, account, dashboard, and customer-admin journeys: semantic structure, labeled inputs, keyboard operation, visible focus, adequate contrast, screen-reader status/error announcements, responsive reflow, accessible dialogs/tables, and chart text/table alternatives. Do not use color alone to communicate status or block password-manager paste/autofill.
- Validate core flows on narrow screens and desktop with keyboard and assistive-technology checks plus the smallest automated accessibility checks. Test whether a new user can onboard, understand a finding, choose an action, and find trial/billing information; a component library alone is not proof of accessibility or usability.

D113 lets downstream source-scoped report and first-value implementation reuse a locally checked text-first project dashboard (`P08-06a`) while the mandatory human keyboard/screen-reader/320px–400% reflow walkthrough (`P08-06b`) remains unfinished. UI fixtures, semantic HTML and a successful build never substitute for that evidence or the public-launch accessibility gate.

### 23.6 Navigation And Public/App Boundaries

Group the app around Overview, Opportunities, Audits, Content, Competitors/AI Visibility, and project Integrations/Settings; keep organization Team/Billing and personal Security/Notifications distinguishable. Use consistent breadcrumbs/back navigation and explain permission-dependent controls. This is an information-architecture baseline to validate with wireframes, not a requirement to expose every future feature at launch.

Provide clear transitions from public pricing/demo/help pages into authentication/onboarding and back to relevant help. Use allowlisted internal return routes rather than arbitrary post-login redirects. Public demos must be synthetic or explicitly approved and isolated from private customer artifacts; sharing a report requires the access model in Section 29.1, not an indexable public bucket.

### 23.7 Static Rendering And Public Page Contract

- Serve the build-time-prerendered public routes and non-indexable CSR account/application routes from the same intended `organicseoops.com` static frontend. Route inventory and CDN behavior, not a separate app hostname, distinguish those spaces. Django is intended at `api.organicseoops.com`; configure exact origins, cookies, CORS and CSRF only against acquired/deployed hosts in P13-01.
- D106 keeps browser API/auth calls same-origin on the public static host: explicit CloudFront path behaviors proxy `/api/...` and `/_allauth/...` (including OAuth browser callbacks) to the separate HTTPS Django origin, with all cookies and CSRF-capable methods forwarded and dynamic caching disabled. D107 clarifies that these paths forward the **public viewer Host**, because OAuth exact redirect validation and host-only session/CSRF cookies must see the browser URL at Django; the origin TLS certificate must cover both public and API names, and Django permits only those exact hosts. Under D108 signed provider webhooks also use the canonical root-host proxied API path, not an assumed direct API-host bypass. This path proxy does not turn unknown public routes or assets into API/CSR fallbacks; real Host/Origin/CORS/CSRF/TLS behavior remains a P13-01 hosted gate.
- D108 narrows the API origin ingress: only CloudFront's managed origin-facing IP ranges reach the HTTPS ALB, and its backend EC2 proxy port is reachable only from that ALB in the private VPC. Forward the public viewer Host and CloudFront-generated viewer address on explicit API/auth/Admin/Admin-static paths; the proxy rejects ordinary requests missing that derived viewer identity and supplies safe HTTPS/client-IP forwarding. MFA Admin and signed Paddle webhooks use the canonical root-host proxied paths; direct API-host webhooks are no longer assumed reachable. Public/app static paths remain on S3/OAC. Actual ALB headers, TLS/SAN, SG, client-rate-limit behavior and webhook signature must be verified on deployed hosts.
- The local router now reuses the account/workspace lazy module pattern for public descriptors, with private prefix precedence and leaf-controlled explicit public locale. Current thirty-six noindex development routes remain unapproved, including paired local public/help/legal drafts; initial298.06kB stays under the unchanged300kB warning after the measured root/copy reduction and removal of mutable approval records from hashed assets. Approved-route metadata and matching static/CDN inventory are locally fixture-checked; actual content/bilingual/publication and hosted evidence remain gated.
- Define a route inventory identifying indexable public pages to prerender, non-indexable CSR account/application routes, public redirects, and not-found behavior. Parameterized public routes must have an explicit build-time URL inventory; do not rely on an SSR fallback for unbuilt paths.
- Use the selected Angular release's supported fully static output and route-level prerender/client modes. Current official guidance documents `outputMode: static`, `RenderMode.Prerender`, and `RenderMode.Client`; verify compatibility at scaffold time rather than pinning versions here. Build-time rendering packages may be used without deploying a Node server.
- Generated public HTML must contain meaningful primary content, headings, real crawlable links, titles/descriptions, canonical/social metadata, and truthful applicable structured data before browser JavaScript executes. Keep content consistent through hydration; do not hide the landing content behind API calls or a loading screen. Private account data, tokens, and credentialed responses must never enter public build artifacts.
- Use responsive optimized images with dimensions, sensible font loading, limited scripts, and optional reduced-motion-safe animation. Define and check route-specific HTML/CSS/JavaScript/image budgets during implementation; prerendering alone does not guarantee good Core Web Vitals or eliminate browser JavaScript.
- Public content and pricing changes require a rebuild/redeploy and appropriate HTML/CDN cache refresh. Include all generated public pages in validation and deployment, not only the root `index.html`; validate that published pricing matches the active offer.
- Verify generated files and the actual CDN behavior: direct deep links, JavaScript-disabled public content, metadata/sitemap consistency, canonical-host/path redirects, real public 404 responses, missing assets, and CSR application refresh. These checks run in CI/staging before launch; noindex is never a substitute for authentication.

## 24. Docker And Local Infrastructure

The repository will eventually be a monorepo:

```text
organicseoops/
├── frontend/
├── backend/
├── deploy/
├── docs/
├── .agents/
├── AGENTS.md
├── CLAUDE.md
├── README.md
├── TODO.md
├── compose.yaml
└── .env.example
```

Application directories and Compose configuration are created by authorized Phase 1 tasks; consult the implementation checkpoint for actual paths and verification.

Local source development uses an explicitly selected development Compose override,
separate project/image tags, private generated credentials, dedicated volumes and
a distinct loopback proxy port. Django source paths are mounted into the reusable
backend dependency image so API edits reload without rebuilding; worker and Beat
code changes require explicit process restart. PostgreSQL/Redis remain unpublished
on the host, migrations remain explicit, and ordinary development commands must
not attach to or recreate legacy/verification projects.

### 24.1 Separate Containers

Environment ownership (D062): keep the secret-free `.env.example` at repository
root, private local `backend/.env`, private production `backend/.env.prod`, and
isolated check files `backend/.env.ci` / `.env.check-TASK`. Select backend files
explicitly via Compose; never regenerate credentials against existing volumes.
Frontend public configuration uses `frontend/src/environments/environment.ts`
for local development and `environment.prod.ts` through Angular production file
replacement. These browser files contain public relative API/auth paths, never
backend credentials. Production placeholders do not establish deployment readiness.

Direct host Django development (D063) may load `backend/.env` automatically when
no complete process environment is supplied. Explicit values win and production
does not auto-load the local file. The development-only Compose override exposes
its isolated PostgreSQL and Redis on loopback ports15432/16379 through a separate
host-access bridge. The base/production services retain their private topology;
the installed host PostgreSQL5432 is not reused automatically.

The planned EC2 Compose services are:

```text
organicseoops-proxy
organicseoops-api
organicseoops-worker-default
organicseoops-worker-ai
organicseoops-worker-crawl
organicseoops-worker-audit
organicseoops-beat
organicseoops-postgres
organicseoops-redis
```

PostgreSQL, Redis, Django API, each worker class, scheduler, and reverse proxy are separate containers.

The API, workers, and scheduler may reuse the same immutable backend image. Reusing an image does not combine the processes into one container.

An optional frontend development container may be provided through a Compose profile. The production frontend does not run in EC2.

### 24.2 Persistence And Networking

- PostgreSQL data uses an encrypted persistent EBS-backed volume initially.
- Redis uses a separate volume/configuration if broker persistence is required.
- PostgreSQL and Redis expose no public host ports in production.
- Only the reverse proxy or load balancer reaches the public network.
- Services communicate through private Docker networks.
- Health checks must exist for deployable services.
- S3 stores uploads, large artifacts, exports, and backups.

## 25. Frontend Deployment

The frontend deployment source is the existing TransferTakip and AirportTransfer pattern, updated for OrganicSEOOps.

The accepted production path is:

```text
GitHub Actions
→ install with npm ci
→ run checks
→ build prerendered public HTML and CSR application assets
→ upload to S3
→ invalidate CloudFront
```

AWS CodeBuild is not part of the accepted frontend plan.

### 25.1 GitHub Actions Requirements

Prepare and locally validate the workflows, IAM/OIDC templates, static routing rules and operator runbooks without a live GitHub remote, AWS account, owned domain or deployment. Actual hosted Actions execution, DNS/TLS/IAM verification, migrations and production exposure are separate explicitly authorized release operations.

- Trigger on the production branch and relevant frontend/workflow paths.
- Use the Node version compatible with the selected Angular release.
- Use `npm ci`, not `npm install`.
- Run the smallest reliable frontend checks before deployment.
- Build with the explicit production configuration.
- Authenticate to AWS through GitHub OIDC and an IAM role where possible.
- Avoid long-lived AWS credentials in GitHub secrets.
- Read bucket name, CloudFront distribution ID, region, and public API URL from environment-specific configuration.
- Upload all generated HTML, including nested prerendered pages and the CSR entry document, with no-cache/revalidation headers.
- Upload content-hashed JavaScript and CSS with long immutable caching.
- Do not apply immutable caching to runtime configuration or non-versioned files.
- Invalidate changed HTML/runtime configuration at CloudFront after a successful upload, including nested prerendered routes.
- Fail the workflow on upload or invalidation failure.

### 25.2 Static Public Pages And CSR Hosting Requirements

- CloudFront serves the S3 origin.
- The intended canonical frontend host is `organicseoops.com` for public, account and application routes; the intended API host is `api.organicseoops.com`. Domain acquisition, certificates, DNS and environment-specific trusted-origin configuration remain P13-01 deployment work.
- Under D106–D108, explicitly forward `/api/*` and `/_allauth/*` from the canonical static browser host to the API HTTPS origin with no caching, all required methods/headers/cookies and the validated **public viewer Host** for exact OAuth/session/CSRF URLs; forward MFA Admin and Admin static on dedicated behaviors and signed webhook requests on the canonical root API path. The origin TLS certificate must cover both public and API hosts and an ALB/private proxy boundary must restrict direct API-host access. Production frontend uses relative browser paths. A cross-origin fetch rewrite without a new CSRF/cookie contract is not an equivalent deployment.
- HTTPS is mandatory.
- Serve each known public route's generated HTML. Restrict CSR entry-document rewrites to the configured application/account route space; a blanket 403/404-to-200 SPA fallback must not hide missing public pages or assets.
- Handle canonical-host/path and retired-URL redirects with real HTTP redirects at the hosting/CDN layer; Angular prerender redirect metadata is not a replacement for intentional 301/308 responses. Provide a genuine public 404 status with a helpful static error page.
- All generated HTML and runtime configuration must refresh on deployment; preserve immutable caching only for content-hashed assets.
- The authenticated application should use `noindex` metadata and suitable response headers where practical.
- Static application files being downloadable is not a security boundary; Django protects all data and operations.

## 26. Backend Deployment

The planned backend path is:

```text
GitHub Actions
→ test backend
→ build backend Docker image
→ push image to Amazon ECR
→ deploy to EC2 through SSM or controlled SSH
→ pull images
→ run migrations as an explicit step
→ start/update Compose services
→ verify health
```

The deployment should not place a GitHub personal access token in a remote `git pull` URL.

Deployment must account for:

- Migration ordering
- Backup before risky migrations
- One-off administrative commands
- Worker restart behavior
- Beat singleton behavior
- Health verification
- Rollback of application images
- The fact that database migrations may not always be automatically reversible

Django static files needed by Django Admin can be collected into the backend image and served using an appropriate production mechanism.

## 27. AWS And Secrets

Initial AWS components:

- EC2 for backend containers
- EBS for persistent PostgreSQL data
- ECR for backend images
- S3 for frontend, artifacts, uploads, and backups using separate buckets or prefixes as appropriate
- CloudFront for frontend delivery
- IAM roles for EC2 and GitHub Actions
- Secrets Manager or SSM Parameter Store for deployment secrets where appropriate
- CloudWatch for logs and basic alarms

Infrastructure credentials and bootstrap encryption/IAM identities are private deployment configuration, not customer-editable provider keys. Implement deployable templates/runbooks and local topology/restore checks without provisioning AWS; record unrun hosted checks for release.
- SES or a selected transactional-email service before public registration/trial launch

User-supplied provider keys and Google OAuth refresh tokens require encryption at rest, strict access, rotation/revocation support, and redacted administration displays. Platform-owned AWS access should use IAM roles rather than static keys.

S3 data containing imports, customer metrics, research artifacts, or exports must remain private and use authorized API access or short-lived signed URLs. The public frontend bucket/origin is a separate concern.

## 28. Security Requirements

- HTTPS only in production
- Secure, HttpOnly authentication cookies
- CSRF enforcement
- Strict CORS allowlist
- Login and sensitive-action throttling
- Email verification
- Strong password validation and reset flow
- Staff MFA, session revocation, secure email changes, and last-owner protection
- Tenant-scoped querysets and object-level authorization
- Automated tests for cross-organization access
- No secrets in source control, logs, task arguments, or frontend bundles
- Encrypted sensitive credentials
- Redacted provider errors where they may contain secrets
- Private PostgreSQL and Redis
- SSRF protections for crawling and remote fetches
- Upload type and size validation
- S3 ownership prefixes and access checks
- Audit logging for administrative and sensitive actions
- Verified ownership before sensitive domain operations
- Dependency and container vulnerability checks in CI when scaffolding begins
- D055's initial check-only CI uses npm audit plus Trivy lock/image vulnerability checks, failing on HIGH/CRITICAL findings or scanner errors; explicit local exceptions do not silently suppress findings or replace production release gates
- Verified billing webhooks, server-side entitlement/usage enforcement, and authorization on exports/report shares

## 29. Observability And Operations

The platform should eventually expose enough operational information to answer:

- Which jobs are failing and why?
- Which provider is slow or rate limited?
- What did a project spend today or this month?
- Which scheduled syncs are stale?
- Is the crawler overloading a host?
- Are backups completing?
- Are API, worker, PostgreSQL, Redis, and disk healthy?

Initial operations should include:

- Structured application logs
- Correlation through job and request identifiers
- Container health checks
- Job duration and failure metrics
- Provider usage and error records
- Disk and memory alarms
- PostgreSQL backup status
- Daily backups to private S3 with retention
- Restore instructions and periodic restore testing

A large observability stack is not required initially. CloudWatch and application records are sufficient until scale justifies more tooling.

### 29.1 Customer Notifications, Reports, And Support

- Deliver verification/recovery/invitation/security messages and trial-expiry/usage/payment notices with clear actions. Configure production transactional email, sender authentication (SPF/DKIM/DMARC as applicable), retries, bounce/complaint handling, and delivery diagnostics before public onboarding depends on it. Redact tokens and sensitive payloads in logs.
- D104 selects Amazon SES authenticated STARTTLS SMTP as the initial transactional sender and a private SNS→SQS subscription for bounce/complaint feedback; D105 corrects the EC2 IAM isolation contract after marked review. Restrict SES→SNS publishing to the exact verified identity/account and SNS→SQS delivery to that topic. Queue IAM access requires a genuinely separate default-worker workload identity/host: a shared EC2 instance profile for a multi-container Compose host is not worker-only IAM isolation. The normal app host remains unable to read/delete the feedback queue; a separate worker host with private DB/Redis connectivity may run the default worker with its own narrowly scoped role when P13's templates/host checks are ready. No public SNS callback or unverified certificate URL is trusted. Match bounded SES feedback to an exact stored email address (even if verification/primary status is pending), then suppress sending only for the currently verified address; another verified address can support recovery. API and default worker alone receive SMTP credentials. Sender/domain identity, SPF/DKIM/DMARC, private inter-host networking, queue policy/IAM, actual SES sandbox/production delivery and feedback are release evidence, not inferred from local fixtures. P3A-10's marked security review covers this incoming trust boundary before local card acceptance.
- D105's local split-host/private-network/IAM and release/rollback templates are accepted at P13-01/P13-03. P3A-10 therefore retains reviewed bilingual critical copy and actual SES sender/domain/delivery/feedback plus hosted identity/network denial as its remaining gates; the historical local split-host implementation blocker is superseded. Local templates do not establish hosted isolation or email delivery.
- Provide weekly summaries/scheduled reports and alerts for meaningful SEO regressions, broken/stale integrations, important job failures, and supported AI-visibility changes. Include source freshness and limitations; noisy or low-sample fluctuations should not be marketed as proven regressions.
- Store per-user/project channel/frequency preferences, deduplicate related alerts, and distinguish optional digests/marketing consent from necessary account/security/billing communications. Check recipient membership and permissions at delivery, not just when a schedule is created. Expired entitlements must not trigger new paid analysis to populate a report.
- Support authorized exports and shareable report snapshots with explicit scope, timestamps, and sources. Default to authenticated recipients; any external link needs owner/admin authorization, high-entropy expiring/revocable access, a disclosure of what recipients can see, noindex, and private artifact storage. Removing access or closing an organization must revoke live shares. Avoid raw secrets, customer identifiers, and CSV/formula injection in exported content.
- Provide contextual help, FAQs, an SEO glossary, contact/support requests with safe job/request IDs, and issue status. Use permission-scoped support records and audited staff actions, not routine impersonation or copying customer exports into external tools.
- Show retention/export/closure policies and enforce Section 7.4. Monitor onboarding success, time to first useful result, trial conversion, and recurring usage with minimal appropriate product analytics; these are distinct from customer-site performance metrics.

### 29.2 Acquisition-To-Revenue Measurement

Define an event dictionary for landing-page view, primary CTA, successful registration, email verification, eligible trial activation, first successful import/audit, first accepted action, checkout initiation, and confirmed paid activation. Each definition must record its trigger, visitor/user/organization measurement unit, deduplication key, allowed properties, source of truth, and retention. Do not count invited teammates or repeated callbacks as new organization trials/conversions. Paid activation is verified application/provider state, not a checkout return-page view; report renewals, cancellations, and refunds separately from first conversion.

Agree on conversion denominators, attribution windows, and the first-value definition before interpreting a funnel. Report stage drop-off, time to first useful result, and trial-to-paid cohorts with enough observation time; do not compare an unfinished trial cohort as though every member has had the same chance to convert. Preserve first-party source/campaign evidence where available and label unknown attribution, blocked tracking, consent gaps, and incomplete anonymous-to-account linkage.

Validate attribution across the actual marketing/app subdomains or domains, email verification links, and hosted payment redirects. Avoid replacing original acquisition with self-referrals or payment-return traffic. Use an appropriate consent-aware measurement design for the selected markets; do not introduce fingerprinting or bypass user preferences to fill attribution gaps. Minimize identifiers and strip secrets/personal data from URLs and event properties; never send passwords, verification/reset tokens, or customer SEO exports into marketing analytics. Reconcile authoritative milestone counts with operational records. Product acquisition analytics remains separate from the analytics imported for customer websites; select the analytics tool and consent configuration at implementation time.

#### 29.2.1 Acquisition Event Dictionary `acquisition-v1`

This version defines authoritative milestones without adding an analytics store or emitter. Every future emitted record needs a fixed event ID, dictionary version, one measurement grain, one UTC `occurred_at`, and a server-side deduplication key. The operational primary keys in that key are not event properties or analytics identifiers. The allowed-properties column lists only additional data-minimized fields: names, email addresses, organization/project names, domains, URLs or query strings, provider property IDs, file contents, tokens, customer SEO data and free text are prohibited. Route, CTA, locale, consent and time can still be pseudonymous personal/behavioral data when linked, so consent, retention/deletion and legal review remain required. Until those are selected, no separate event copy exists: available milestones are derived from their owning operational records and follow those records' lifecycle. `P02-06`, legal/privacy review and P3A-11 must still define analytics-specific retention or deletion behavior.

## 30. Scalability Path

The first production deployment may run all backend services on one EC2 instance. This is cost-effective but is a known single point of failure.

The planned evolution is:

```text
PostgreSQL container → Amazon RDS
Redis container → Amazon ElastiCache
Worker containers → separate EC2 instances or ECS services
API container → multiple instances behind an ALB
Compose deployment → ECS or another orchestrator when operational need exists
```

Environment-based connection settings, separate service containers, stateless API behavior, S3 artifacts, and durable PostgreSQL job records make this move possible without rewriting the product.

Kubernetes is not justified initially.

## 31. OpenCode And Repository Remediation

OpenCode is not part of routine SEO research or content generation. Provider APIs should be called directly for those jobs.

A later repository-aware remediation capability may:

```text
Technical finding
→ inspect an explicitly connected repository
→ propose a patch
→ run repository checks
→ create a branch or pull request
→ require human review
```

Requirements for that later capability:

- Explicit repository connection and authorization
- Isolated, temporary execution environment
- Least-privilege repository credentials
- Read-only behavior by default
- No production deployment permission
- Full audit trail
- Secret filtering
- Resource and network limits

D439/P16-01 authorization source is written but untested (2026-10-19): explicit GitHub repository ID/name/commit, read-only GitHub App consent with S256/session/actor/project binding, expiring encrypted user credentials, verified installation permissions, token-specific disconnect and signed revocation events. Existing private native controls and owner closure/deletion recovery include unknown exchange handling without fabricating provider confirmation. No automatic renewal or code access/execution occurs during consent. Source migration0021 is unapplied. [D439](DECISIONS.md#d439-read-only-github-repository-consent-before-isolated-analysis).

D440 adds the initial explicitly acknowledged root dependency-manifest collector, dedicated repository queue/controller, disposable offline parser, private history/cancel/results/API/native screen and separate-host deployment artifacts. It uses only root package.json/pyproject.toml/requirements.txt at the exact commit, <=128KiB each/100 declarations, with blob/SHA256 provenance and no repository scripts/installers/agent. A dedicated rootless Docker/cgroupv2 host runs the trusted controller container; each disposable non-root runner has no network/socket/credentials/host mounts, read-only root and bounded CPU/time/PID/memory/tmpfs/output. Controller requires private verifiedTLS DB/Redis and disabled mail. At most13 fixed-host GitHub reads are durably charged as attempted internal units; fresh source/authority/cancel/entitlement checks surround collection/delivery. Durable exact-identity cleanup must confirm removal before terminal status/usage settlement; uncertainty blocks further project analysis/deletion and recovery never repeats collection. Raw manifests are temporary; only filtered structured declarations and provenance are retained. No installed versions/vulnerabilities/recursive code/whole-repository analysis is claimed. Broader authorized dependency/code scanning, P16-02 agent patches/checks/PRs and all acceptance remain unfinished. Default intake is disabled until actual isolation/security evidence is configured. No tests/checks/migrations/runtime/provider/repository/Docker operation ran under D437; bounded advisory source reviews are not the marked Security gate. [D440](DECISIONS.md#d440-bounded-offline-repository-manifest-analysis-on-a-dedicated-rootless-host), [operator preparation](REPOSITORY_ANALYSIS.md).

It reuses D440 parsing and confirmed cleanup, then sends only filtered ecosystem/name/version metadata to fixed `api.osv.dev` after a separate explicit disclosure acknowledgement. One batch<=50 unique pins, maximum14 internal attempted-request units (13GitHub+1OSV), bounded IDs/modified timestamps/receipt/fingerprint, unsupported/excluded/paginated/truncated coverage and original source-authority delivery are preserved in API/native/export.
