## 32. Agent Guidance And Project Knowledge


The repository uses:

- `AGENTS.md` for always-applicable project instructions
- `CLAUDE.md` as a thin adapter that imports `AGENTS.md` for Claude Code
- `.agents/rules/` for task-specific requirements referenced by the root guide or a skill
- `.agents/skills/` for provider-neutral Agent Skills-compatible workflows
- `.agents/references/` for concise supporting context and source maps
- `.agents/roles/` for provider-neutral read-only reviewer instructions
- `.opencode/agents/` for thin OpenCode reviewer registration and tool permissions
- `.codex/agents/` and `.claude/agents/` for thin native registrations of the same shared reviewer roster (D114)
- `docs/PROJECT_PLAN.md` for the canonical plan
- `docs/DECISIONS.md` for accepted architectural and product decisions
- `TODO.md` for the persistent actionable backlog
- `docs/IMPLEMENTATION_GUIDE.md` for coding contracts, dependency ordering, and cross-model start/resume instructions
- `docs/IMPLEMENTATION_TASKS.md` for stable task IDs, prerequisites, implementation instructions, and acceptance checks
- `docs/IMPLEMENTATION_STATE.md` for current authorization/scope, active substeps, actual paths/commands, verification evidence, blockers, and the exact next action
- Session todo tools only for work within one active session

The repository includes a documentation-maintenance skill and eight read-only reviewers: `seo-reviewer` for SEO assumptions and workflows; `plan-reviewer` for architecture, sequencing, and document consistency; and `django-tenancy-reviewer`, `angular-ui-reviewer`, `jobs-integrations-reviewer`, `billing-entitlements-reviewer`, `security-reviewer`, and `deployment-reviewer` for their bounded specialist areas (D045). Authoritative role instructions live in `.agents/roles/`; thin OpenCode, Codex, and Claude adapters register the same roster (D114). OpenCode retains its explicit read/search/public-documentation-fetch allowlist and denied edit, shell, and nested delegation permissions. Codex uses a read-only sandbox plus explicit behavioral prohibitions; Claude allows only read/search/public-documentation-fetch tools. OpenCode and Codex routine Angular review uses fixed GPT-5.6 Luna with `low` reasoning (D047); security uses fixed GPT-6 Luna with `medium` reasoning and SEO uses fixed GPT-6 Luna with `high` reasoning (D133); the other four specialists and `plan-reviewer` use fixed GPT-6.1 Sol with `medium` reasoning (D430, superseding D424). These OpenCode/Codex assignments are independent of the invoking primary model; Claude adapters inherit the invoking Claude model. Reports inform the main agent's work; reviewers do not apply changes, run tests, call operational APIs, or conduct active scans. Shared roles route current source ownership and verified command families while explicitly reporting missing, unrun, provider-blocked, or production-only evidence.

D376 makes those reviews usable across hosts: native file readers/search are preferred; a narrow non-mutating command-reading fallback is allowed where no reader exists. All reviewer roles follow AGENTS.md Reviewer file access, retaining no edits/tests/scans/operational calls/secrets/delegation. Each review establishes actual file/symbol/line evidence and lists unread required dependencies. Unavailable source access is not a clean review; parent fallback stays explicitly separate and independent review remains pending before release. Native adapter configuration alone is not proof of effective read-only isolation.

Under D324, the main agent proactively invokes necessary bounded specialist reviews for required gates or substantive risks/decisions without a separate user prompt; D047 still excludes an automatic full-roster review after every change. The main agent handles routine consistency/configuration checks. Reserve deeper reviews for architecture, tenancy, billing/concurrency, job reliability, security, and production/recovery milestones. Security implementation review is an explicit task-ledger allowlist (D077): only cards marked **Security review gate**, or a direct user request, invoke `security-reviewer`; each marked gate gets one post-focused-check review, routine remediation is handled by the main agent, and a repeat requires an unresolved critical finding or material trust-boundary redesign recorded in the checkpoint. Unmarked cards never invoke it merely because implementation continues. Complex Angular rendering, authentication, accessibility, or unresolved cross-layer behavior should be flagged to the parent, which can request a deeper existing plan/tenancy/deployment review with the Angular role criteria supplied; security escalation follows D077. Reviewers cannot delegate; this adds no automatic model-switching framework or extra agent.

The built-in OpenCode `explore` subagent is assigned `openai/gpt-5.6-luna` with `low` reasoning in the project `opencode.json` for cost-conscious file discovery, reference tracing, and codebase summaries (D044). This model-only override retains the built-in exploration prompt and permissions.

Role instructions are portable, but runtime registration and permissions are host-specific. OpenCode, Codex, and Claude adapters explicitly load the same role files; other tools can do so directly or add a thin adapter. `.agents/roles/` is not assumed to be universally auto-discovered. Usage and validation commands are documented in `.agents/README.md`. Adding reviewers does not authorize application implementation. Existing reviewer context is grounded in current source paths and checks; add implementation skills or write-capable coding specialists only when a concrete recurring workflow demonstrates that need. Agent resources follow these rules:

- Shared project knowledge should remain provider-neutral.
- Provider-specific adapters should stay thin and point to shared files rather than duplicating them.
- Arbitrary rule and reference directories must be explicitly routed from `AGENTS.md` or a matching skill because tools do not discover them uniformly.
- Specialist subagents should be read-only by default.
- Project-approved model assignments belong in host adapters; credentials and personal model preferences must remain outside the repository.
- Free model agents must be explicit opt-in and must not receive confidential data by default.

Every future creation or modification of agent instructions, agents, subagents, skills, or references follows the mandatory project-root `AGENTS.md` Universal Agent Resource Maintenance workflow (D115): canonical shared Markdown, direct-reading fallback, matching core-role registrations across OpenCode/Codex/Claude, host-only settings in adapters, synchronized routing/documentation, and verified references and imports. Document genuine host-specific exceptions explicitly.

Primary-agent model and reasoning selection stays with the coding tool's current session or personal defaults (D116); do not pin either in project configs or primary-agent adapters unless the user explicitly requests a project override. Specialist subagent model/reasoning assignments remain explicit and independent.

D431/D436 record the user-requested host exception: the existing one-minute Codex continuation launcher explicitly starts GPT-6.1 Sol with medium reasoning and disables Fast mode for the main coder and every subagent via CLI overrides (`service_tier="default"`, `features.fast_mode=false`). D436 supersedes D432. Explicit overrides prevent the personal Fast default or a model catalog default from re-enabling Fast; subagents inherit these settings because their role files do not override them. Local configuration checks do not establish actual served tier or speed. Only timer-started fresh sessions receive this selection; personal global/interactive defaults, existing sessions, reviewer routing, permissions, interval, activity/exhaustion guards and retention stay unchanged. Host configuration/checks remain outside the repository, documented in `.agents/README.md` and TOOL-03.

D124 permits a user-requested local OpenCode continuation timer as a host-specific adapter, not a product job scheduler: every ten minutes, skip active agents/subagents or unavailable tracking; otherwise open a fresh terminal/session following `CONTINUE.md` and the durable checkpoint. Scheduled sessions auto-approve asking permissions and choose recommended/default/first options without extending implementation authority. Explicit denies/read-only reviewers remain enforced. Host installation, checks and pause/resume commands are documented in `.agents/README.md` (TOOL-03); shared handoff instructions remain provider-neutral.

D379 restores interactive scheduled Codex terminals on this host: launch `codex PROMPT` with direct inherited stdin/stdout/stderr instead of piping TUI output. Native Codex session history retains the conversation; the private per-run log records a history notice rather than a full screen transcript. The owning wrapper ignores Ctrl+C while the native TUI handles it, records completion, and retains D326 five-window ownership/finished-only cleanup. D434 supersedes D433: idle/input-waiting managed sessions may stay open during a fresh launch; actual coding activity still blocks. D124/D131 activity/startup/overlap/exhaustion controls, one-minute timer checks, D323 personal defaults, D375 verification cadence and project authorization remain unchanged. Reopen a stopped saved automatic session by its explicit ID to retain its conversation, then reconcile the checkpoint and unresolved child checks before continuing. Official [Codex CLI reference](https://learn.chatgpt.com/docs/cli/reference) documents interactive launch and resume.

D434 supersedes D433: idle timer-started sessions and sessions explicitly waiting for user input permit a fresh timer launch; actively coding project sessions/subagents block it. The metadata reader preserves native `activeFlags`; only `active` with exactly `[waitingOnUserInput]` is treated as waiting rather than coding. Approval/unknown/error/unavailable activity remains blocking. Reuse D381's project-wide activity snapshot, 120-second startup grace and verified native-child identity; preserve watcher/startup/D124/D131 guards, one-minute cadence, D431/D436 Sol 6.1/medium/standard-speed flags and finished-only last-five retention. Existing sessions are preserved. This is a launch-time project snapshot, not per-client tracking or protection against later manual resumption; original active-session overlap has not been reproduced or its cause established.

D435 unattended choice rule: timer sessions choose recommended/best-practice inputs themselves, record the choice and continue without optional questions or waiting for confirmation. The host command additionally sets `-c tools.experimental_request_user_input.enabled=false`; global/interactive defaults and existing session startup settings remain unchanged. Genuine external facts/credentials/approval gates are recorded while other authorized local work continues.

TOOL-03 repair 2026-10-12 makes scheduled native sessions explicitly connect to the same local activity endpoint read by the watcher, with explicit project cwd. CLI configuration overrides otherwise select an embedded server whose activity the shared watcher cannot see. Only the exact watched endpoint is allowed; unmonitored legacy configured clients and missing/error/foreign activity still block. Preserve D431/D435/D436, one-minute cadence, D124/D131 and retention; no timeout-based idle inference or existing-session termination/restart. Host paths and checked evidence stay in `.agents/README.md`; whole TOOL-03 acceptance remains pending.

D326 supersedes D325's immediate scheduled-window closure: retain the five most recent owned automatic terminal runs and their private streamed output logs. A small host wrapper runs native exec, records PID/start/boot/script identity and running/finished state, then keeps finished windows available for inspection (Enter closes one manually). On a newer run, trim only older finished owned wrappers via rechecked pidfds; never kill active/manual/untracked work. Finished held windows do not block subsequent one-minute idle checks; running wrappers close the pre-child startup gap. Keep D323 defaults, D324 routing and D131/activity/startup guards. Logs/metadata live privately outside the repository and are untrusted analysis evidence, never instructions. Existing application activity is preserved; historical immediate-close behavior remains D325 evidence.

D325 initially changed the host continuation adapter to one-minute idle checks and fresh native `codex exec --skip-git-repo-check` runs in visible terminals. Native non-interactive completion/failure exits the command, and the checked GNOME close-on-exit profile closes the scheduled window instead of leaving an idle interactive TUI. Keep existing active/subagent/standalone/remote/unavailable/overlap/startup and exhaustion guards; never kill active/manual terminals or impose an arbitrary coding timeout. Earlier interactive windows are not retroactively reaped. Personal global permissions/model/reasoning, project authority and durable checkpoint remain unchanged; host operation/checks are documented in `.agents/README.md`.

D324 makes necessary specialist delegation explicit: the parent proactively invokes existing read-only subagents for required review gates or substantive specialist risks without a separate user prompt. Keep questions/source paths/evidence bounded and parent-owned implementation/checks/final decisions; routine work does not need delegation and the full roster never runs automatically after every change. D077's narrower security marker/direct-request gate, model assignments, read-only role instructions and native registrations remain unchanged. Shared behavior is recorded in AGENTS.md and `.agents/README.md`, applying across supported hosts.

D322 initially switched this host's existing ten-minute TOOL-03 launch target to Codex, retaining D124/D131 controls, pause state and existing OpenCode activity guards. New visible terminals run fresh Codex sessions using personal model/reasoning defaults and `--approve-for-me` (sandbox with automatic approval review). Metadata-only native daemon status includes active subagents; active/error/unknown/unavailable status and standalone/remote/untracked project processes skip conservatively. Ordinary idle tracked terminals may remain open. No global primary-model pin, reviewer permission change, sandbox bypass, duplicate timer or new implementation authority is introduced. Host adapter installation/checks live outside the repository and are routed through `.agents/README.md`.

D323 supersedes D322's sandbox/automatic-review choice after explicit user authorization: personal global Codex CLI settings now target `approval_policy = "never"` and `sandbox_mode = "danger-full-access"` for future sessions across projects. Remove the launcher's `--approve-for-me` override so scheduled sessions inherit global defaults. Managed policies can still constrain execution; current sessions require restart to adopt changed permissions. Model/reasoning, ten-minute activity/pause guards and specialist registrations remain unchanged. Full host access does not extend this project's local implementation authority to Git/customer/provider/production/deployment operations. Configuration remains outside the repository; host checks and status evidence are tracked in TOOL-03 and the checkpoint.

D322 also makes `docs/IMPLEMENTATION_STATE.md` a compact current handoff: replace superseded summaries, preserve active verification/unresolved operations/scope, and link dated same-directory historical evidence. The complete pre-cleanup checkpoint is retained byte-for-byte in `docs/IMPLEMENTATION_STATE_ARCHIVE_2026-10-07.md`; archives are read only for relevant evidence. The comprehensive plan, append-only decisions, card ledger, backlog and all acceptance gates retain their authority.

D131 extends TOOL-03 with an agent-recorded durable exhaustion pause: once no dependency-ready authorized local implementation or automated checks remain, finalize durable documentation and pause repeated launches while leaving live/manual/external gates unfinished. The timer checks the local marker before opening a terminal or invoking a model; explicit resume or changed backlog/continuation guidance/recorded authorization permits reassessment. Routine checkpoint edits do not resume it. Session/context limits, interruptions and absent credentials for fixture-testable work are not exhaustion. Commands, watched inputs and conservative malformed-state handling live in `.agents/README.md`; other hosts retain the shared stopping reason without an implicit timer installation.

Potential future skills, only when repeated implementation work justifies them, include:

- Angular frontend implementation and review
- Django/DRF tenancy and API review
- Job and provider integration review
- SEO analysis and data-quality review
- Deployment and operational review
- Independent security and correctness review

### 32.1 Durable Implementation And Cross-Model Handoff

The implementation guide and task ledger operationalize this canonical plan without replacing its scope or the phase-level backlog (D042). Task prerequisites control execution where phases overlap: commercial decisions can precede jobs, while worker entitlement enforcement, onboarding first value, closure, and public launch require their real supporting features. The internal, public, and AI-visibility release gates remain distinct.

Continuation uses [CONTINUE.md](../CONTINUE.md) as the reusable instruction (D068/D103): finish incomplete work, verify and checkpoint each card, then immediately continue sequentially to the next dependency-ready card within authorized scope. There is no fixed card limit unless the user specifies a narrower task/batch; checkpoints and completed cards alone do not end a session. Build locally testable product/UI/provider/billing and deployment-preparation flows without requesting the user's real credentials, provider accounts, actual domains or deployment access. Distinguish implementation acceptance from live provider, advertised-feature and production-release evidence; absent external evidence remains unverified at its release gate and does not hold up dependent coding that can use a bounded contract fixture. Never waive tenant/security/billing integrity or claim fixtures are live data. Stop at requested scope's completion/user pause, exhaustion of authorized implementation work, or an actual context/tool/session limit, recording the stopping reason and exact next action.

Record explicit implementation authorization and its scope in the checkpoint before coding; preparing these documents does not leave the documentation checkpoint. Git initialization and commits remain separately requested operations. Begin each coding slice by identifying its stable task ID, source context, intended files, and check; update the checkpoint after meaningful edits/checks and before stopping or switching models. Include incomplete/unverified work, actual commands and results, migration/process/external-operation state, blockers, and an exact next step. Do not store secrets or customer payloads in handoffs.

On resume, any model/host reads root guidance, the guide, checkpoint, active task and applicable source context, then reconciles recorded progress with the actual workspace and environment. An interrupted session may leave partially written code or an uncertain external action; verify/reconcile before retrying. Only mark tasks and corresponding TODO items complete after required acceptance is verified. Session tools, model identity, chat memory, reviewer availability, and commits are not prerequisites to file-based continuity. A hard interruption may lose unrecorded work, so on-disk checkpoints and actual-state verification are both required.

## 33. Delivery Roadmap

D429 reactivates the existing phases below for local implementation/automated acceptance after the current R05/R06 automated stage. Respect recorded dependencies and explicit capability/demand conditions; no new product features or suffixes. R06 manual and R07 public-release prerequisites remain separately unfinished; unrelated local coding continues without those external gates.

After foundational data collection, prioritize keyword-to-page mapping, action/change/outcome tracking, and deeper crawl/indexation diagnostics. Analytics-quality context underpins prioritization from the first integration. Assess local SEO early for user-created projects using manual business/profile records; richer local APIs, optional server logs, and authority-provider workflows follow when access and evidence justify them. These capabilities extend existing phases rather than starting a separate platform.

Public SaaS readiness is a cross-cutting launch requirement: the public journey, secure account lifecycle, automatic trials, billing/entitlements, clear accessible UI, notifications, and support must work together before public trial/paid launch. An internal useful SEO release can validate value earlier. Add bounded AI visibility after the core source/evidence and job infrastructure works, then expand based on observed usage. The roadmap below is coarser-grained than the phase breakdown in `TODO.md`; matching milestones have the same dependencies even where their numbers differ.

### Phase 0: Documentation And Foundation Decisions

- Preserve the complete plan and decisions.
- Establish persistent TODO tracking.
- Confirm repository location and naming.
- Do not write application code during the documentation checkpoint.

### Phase 1: Repository And Runtime Scaffold

- Initialize the Git repository.
- Scaffold Angular 22 and Django 6.1 projects using the latest stable compatible releases within those lines; verify dependency compatibility and record exact versions (D041).
- Configure static frontend output with prerendered public routes, CSR account/app routes, and isolated public HTML/CSS versus authenticated UI-library dependencies.
- Add custom Django user model before first migration.
- Add PostgreSQL, Redis, API, worker, Beat, and proxy services as separate containers.
- Add environment templates and health checks.
- Add basic CI checks.

### Phase 2: Authentication, Tenancy, And Superadmin

- Implement registration and email verification.
- Implement login, logout, and password reset.
- Implement organizations, memberships, invitations, and projects.
- Implement project selection and access controls.
- Configure Django Admin for platform operations.
- Add tenant-isolation tests.
- Complete verification/reset/invitation/session/ownership edge cases and the role/action matrix; require staff MFA before production.
- Specify public pages, account/onboarding wireframes, reusable UI conventions, and WCAG 2.2 AA acceptance.
- Add customer organization-admin and permission-scoped platform-support workflows as their resources ship.

### Phase 2A: Commercial Readiness (Public Launch Requirement)

- Decide and record trial duration, eligibility, card/renewal policy, prices/allowances, grace/proration/refund/tax, and retention terms.
- Select the launch audience/positioning, markets/languages, public page/keyword map, proof material, and content ownership/preview/rebuild workflow before final public copy/wireframes.
- Implement automatic verified-organization trial grants, usage/entitlement state, hosted checkout/portal integration, billing events/reconciliation, and owner-facing billing/expiry screens.
- Wire API, scheduler, and worker quota/expiry enforcement as the job/provider phases become available; this milestone is not complete until the end-to-end public journey works.
- Deliver public homepage/features/pricing/demo/help/trust pages, transactional email, essential trial/security/payment notifications, exports/closure, and support access.
- Define and verify acquisition-to-revenue events/cohorts and actual provider access/cost readiness for advertised capabilities; complete the first-party website SEO release checks in Section 34.2.

### Phase 3: Job Platform And AI Configuration

- Implement JobRun, JobStep, artifacts, and status API.
- Configure Celery queues and scheduler.
- Add job progress UI and cancellation.
- Implement provider, model, routing, and usage records.
- Implement OpenAI and OpenAI-compatible adapters.
- Add model routing and budget UI.

### Phase 4: Search And Site Data

- Add Search Console CSV import.
- Add Google OAuth and Search Console synchronization.
- Add GA4 synchronization.
- Add conversion/attribution quality review, branded-query configuration, and local business/profile inventory.
- Establish source-aware webmaster connection and metric handling for later Bing, Yandex, and other engine connectors.
- Add sitemap and robots fetching.
- Add safe project crawler.
- Add selective raw/rendered diagnostics, canonical-target and crawl-depth checks, parameter/pagination handling, structured-data validation, and supported indexation reconciliation.
- Add PageSpeed and CrUX collection.
- Deliver resumable short onboarding, isolated demo data, integration recovery, and a bounded first-results experience.

### Phase 5: SEO Opportunities

- Implement deterministic detectors.
- Add opportunity scoring and evidence.
- Add dashboard and opportunity views.
- Add AI-assisted explanations and prioritization.
- Add reviewable intent clusters and keyword-to-page/action mappings.
- Add assignment, manual implementation/change logs, verification, baselines, and follow-up outcome comparisons with attribution limitations.
- Add early local consistency, profile eligibility, and review-opportunity assessments from available evidence.
- Add clear dashboard states, authorized report exports/shares, weekly summaries, and preference-aware deduplicated alerts.

### Phase 6: Topics, Research, And Content

- Add configurable trend and feed sources.
- Add topic normalization and scoring.
- Add sourced research jobs.
- Add content brief generation.
- Add article drafting, revisions, approval, and export.
- Add content inventory, expertise/fact review, refresh/consolidation/retirement proposals, and links to mapped intent and outcome tracking.
- Add social repurposing drafts.

### Phase 7: Competitors And Expanded Integrations

- Add approved competitors.
- Add SERP provider integration.
- Add public competitor analysis.
- Add optional paid keyword/backlink providers.
- Add keyword volume/difficulty research, scheduled engine/location/device rank tracking, optional competitor traffic estimates, and historical snapshots with coverage labels.
- Add Bing Webmaster Tools and Yandex Webmaster connectors after core Google integrations, gated by supported capabilities and access.
- Add social account analytics where API access permits.
- Add supported local profile/review and location-specific visibility integrations.
- Add authority-building and link-recovery opportunities with manual outreach.
- Add optional authorized bot-log imports and coverage-aware diagnostics after core indexation reconciliation.

### Phase 7A: Bounded AI Visibility

- Select supported collection surfaces and a small declared platform/prompt scope; add versioned neutral/branded prompt sets and budgeted recurring samples.
- Preserve answer/citation evidence, model/surface/mode/market metadata, sample status, and correction history; compute coverage-aware mention/citation metrics deterministically.
- Add competitor/source observations, like-for-like history, identifiable AI-referral analytics, and discoverability recommendations without claiming global chat visibility or guaranteed inclusion.

### Phase 8: Publishing And Scheduling

- Add CMS connections only for requested systems.
- Add approval-controlled publishing.
- Add social scheduling where platform permissions permit.
- Track published URLs and performance changes.

### Phase 9: Usage-Based Refinement And Scale

- Refine existing plans, allowances, provider costs, and retention from real usage; trial/billing launch controls belong to Phase 2A.
- Move PostgreSQL and Redis to managed services when justified.
- Scale API and workers independently.

### Phase 10: Repository-Aware Remediation

- Connect repositories with explicit authorization.
- Run isolated code analysis.
- Propose patches and pull requests.
- Preserve mandatory human review and deployment separation.

## 34. First Useful Release

D426 defines the basic R01–R06 flow. D429 finishes its current automated stage first, then continues all already-planned local contracts; an unavailable manual walkthrough remains pending while unrelated coding proceeds. Real-provider/public evidence stays separate at R07.

The first useful SEO release should support:

```text
Authenticated user
→ organization and project
→ Search Console import or sync
→ project crawl and performance data
→ prioritized, evidenced opportunities
→ intent-to-page mapping and chosen action
→ selected opportunity research
→ sourced content brief
```

An article editor and social repurposing can follow immediately after that flow is stable. Automatic publication is not required to validate product value.

The first opportunity release should also support assignment, manual change logging, technical verification, and baseline/follow-up measurement as sufficient data accumulates. Show unavailable or inconclusive outcomes honestly. Early local SEO assessment can use manually supplied business details without requiring a local API connection.

### 34.1 Public SaaS Launch Acceptance

Before inviting customers into public trials or paid plans, verify the whole flow from truthful public pricing through registration, email verification, exactly-once eligible trial activation, resumable onboarding, bounded first findings, upgrade/payment recovery, cancellation, retained access/export, and closure. Include organization/admin permissions, staff MFA, production email, accessibility, support, and enforced budgets. A trial timer or checkout success page alone does not satisfy this gate. Internal product validation does not require every expanded integration.

AI-visibility marketing must match actually available collection surfaces and sample coverage. Its release gate includes evidence-linked observations, defined denominators, failure/unavailable states, clear API-versus-consumer labeling, and referral-attribution limits, not a claim to observe all chats.

P11A-05's [AI visibility readiness record](AI_VISIBILITY_READINESS.md) separates the checked D120 local journey from unrun provider/privacy/legal/hosted/manual-accessibility release evidence. The existing provider-readiness/public-route guard requires separate declared initial surface and dated feature evidence beyond general AI-text provider readiness. It cannot approve unsupported consumer/citation/referral/scheduled/geolocation/training surfaces; exact-copy human review must still verify prose truth. Local implementation acceptance does not complete GATE-AI or conditional P11A-02s/P11A-04r source requirements.

### 34.2 First-Party Website SEO And Provider Readiness

OrganicSEOOps' own public website needs the same evidence discipline as managed customer sites. Before public launch:

- Approve the actual audience/positioning and page map, available-feature/pricing claims, sample report, publication owners, and chosen locale coverage. Case studies require their own evidence/approval when published; measured case studies are not a prerequisite to a truthful demo-led launch.
- Verify ownership of the final product domain in the relevant webmaster platforms, including Google Search Console and Bing Webmaster Tools where applicable, and submit its production sitemap. Add other engines when selected markets justify them; manual webmaster setup does not depend on implementing every customer connector.
- Test representative public URLs at the real CDN: rendered HTML/metadata, robots/noindex state, canonical/locale links, crawlable navigation, schema where applicable, redirects, public 404s, assets, and mobile/accessibility behavior. Keep staging/preview access restricted and ensure production does not inherit staging noindex directives. Verify the acquisition funnel and consent behavior against the real public/app/payment routing.
- Record an owner, baseline, and review cadence for post-launch sitemap/indexation coverage, broken links, availability, search/referral trends, and real-user performance when sufficient data exists. Missing CrUX or indexation data is unknown, not a pass; filing a sitemap does not guarantee indexing. Track findings through the existing change/outcome workflow. If the product domain is managed inside OrganicSEOOps, use its own isolated project rather than mixing it with either initial domain.
- For each advertised integration, record the actual provider/product or collection surface, supported features, account/access status, required OAuth/app approval where applicable, scopes, applicable usage/redistribution/retention terms, quotas, current cost assumptions, owner, and dated validation evidence. Keep credentials outside these records. Test a representative sync/job and disconnection/rate-limit behavior before claiming availability; an adapter plan or API key alone is not production readiness.
- Set commercial allowances against the supported crawl/AI/provider budget and declared AI-visibility sampling cadence. Complete required access/approvals before relying on an integration for the launch promise, or clearly label its unavailable/planned state and support a truthful alternative workflow. Select concrete AI-visibility platforms and distinguish API sampling from consumer observations as required by Section 17.2.

D420/P13-05a selects a local, secret-free first-party monitoring template and offline coverage report. Actual product origin, owner, cadence and baseline remain unconfigured; dated environment/evidence-kind references distinguish fixtures from human and hosted observations. Its coverage includes public indexability/links/availability, submission versus indexing, search/referrals, lab versus field, mobile/accessibility, approved claims, consent/public/app/email/payment handoffs, the existing public-analytics healthy cleanup deadline and content rollback. Reuse publication/provider readiness records; the report exposes gaps and always leaves release verification to the original gates. This adds no fetch, collector, scheduler, vendor, production configuration or activation. Under D423, local offline preparation may proceed independently of pending P11 full-suite acceptance; actual publication/provider release gates remain unchanged. The [site-monitoring record and offline report](SITE_MONITORING.md) now pass local owning checks and bounded Deployment source review; all real configuration remains unconfigured and every report states RELEASE UNVERIFIED.

## 35. Quality And Acceptance Principles

Every non-trivial implementation phase should include the smallest meaningful automated checks.

D427 makes test additions proportional: inspect existing owning coverage first; reuse it when it verifies the changed contract, and add/extend cases only for concrete uncovered behavior or risk. A brief gap/reason in the active card/checkpoint is enough; no test/spec per edit, ritual red-first cycle, duplicate layer assertions, coverage target or speculative edge-case expansion. Trivial/behavior-preserving edits use relevant static/type/manual checks. New untested non-trivial logic and actual failures still need meaningful automated coverage, with auth/tenant/billing/quota/concurrency/SSRF/migration/destructive-data/input-trust/approval boundaries preserved. Stop when that changed contract/risk is covered; do not delete/weaken assertions or expand speculative coverage. D429 roadmap contracts receive relevant needed checks; full candidate validation covers the current basic stage and final full-local closure.

D375 changes check timing to reduce repeated coding overhead: quick syntax/type checks and focused changed-behavior tests during coding; affected regression once at coherent feature completion; full backend/frontend/integration/security suites and combined production-build/isolated-stack acceptance at app-ready validation or explicit user request. Small suffixed cards are not separate broad-suite milestones. Authentication/tenancy/billing/SSRF/data-loss and migration-safety checks remain focused prerequisites before dependent work or state changes. Build earlier only for concrete dependency/configuration/rendering needs. Preserve every acceptance check but record deferred command/scope and feature/app-ready trigger; required unrun acceptance stays unfinished, with subsequent local coding permitted over implemented, focused-checked interfaces and no known failing contract. Full checks remain necessary before release-ready claims. AGENTS.md and the implementation guide carry the execution policy.

D423 clarifies D375: full suites belong only to an explicitly selected final app-ready/release validation phase or explicit user request. New sessions, timer launches, repairs and unfinished full-validation cards never trigger a full run. Record source/configuration target, scope, reason and owner, and reuse evidence until relevant changes invalidate it. Under D426, default continuation defers full acceptance only while R01–R05 core implementation remains; it automatically selects R06 when those outcomes have focused evidence. Optional expansion cannot postpone final validation. Reconcile an existing broad run once, protect its actual source/database/service dependencies, then continue independent work without progress polling; D428 permits the same-session terminal-receipt wait only after independent core work is exhausted. Collect genuine terminal evidence. Known failed contracts block their actual dependents, and required full acceptance remains unfinished before release.

D425 makes check selection explicit: command maps and old card/checkpoint receipts are coverage references, not instructions to replay them. Choose exact changed-behavior methods/cases or one genuinely small owning file; `quicktest` does not make app-wide labels focused. Reuse passing evidence for unchanged source/configuration. Consult recorded runtimes before expensive checks, narrow to the affected contract and defer non-gating slow coverage to its coherent-feature/final-validation trigger. Documentation-only and trivial changes need only relevant static checks. Run system/migration checks, worker reloads, runtime probes and builds only when their actual boundary changes or a required state-changing gate needs them. Preserve sensitive-boundary checks and known-failure gates; never silently skip coverage, weaken assertions or record deferred acceptance passed.

Under D103, distinguish implementation acceptance (source plus tenant/security/billing-contract tests, UI checks, locally simulated provider failures, migration/topology/restore checks where relevant) from operational/provider/launch acceptance (real account or sandbox evidence, approved domain, hosted deployment and live data). An absent credential, unacquired domain or unavailable AWS account never by itself blocks implementing the corresponding code or its dependent local workflows; it leaves the external evidence explicitly unrun at its release gate. Never activate a public offer, claim a provider works in production, or perform real customer-site/provider/deployment actions from fixtures alone.

Critical test areas include:

- Authentication and account lifecycle
- Verification/reset token replay/expiry, invitation races, session revocation, staff MFA recovery, and last-owner/ownership-transfer invariants
- Organization and project isolation
- Permission boundaries
- Role/action matrix, organization billing isolation, exactly-once trial grants, concurrent quota reservations, expiry during queued/running work, and usage settlement
- Billing signature/replay checks, duplicate/out-of-order events, reconciliation, checkout/renewal/failure/cancellation/downgrade recovery using provider sandbox scenarios
- Job idempotency and state transitions
- Provider fallback and usage accounting
- Import validation
- Multi-engine metric provenance, capability gating, sync isolation, and unavailable-versus-zero data handling
- SEO detector calculations
- Raw/rendered and canonical diagnostics, bounded parameter crawling, and indexation unknown/conflicting states
- Intent/page mapping scope, action approval/state history, and verification separate from outcome claims
- Baseline/window calculations, zero/missing data, branded-query coverage, and analytics-quality context
- Structured-data validation versus rich-result eligibility and content retirement approval boundaries
- SSRF protections
- Content approval boundaries
- AI-visibility alias/citation extraction, valid-sample denominators, failed/unsupported observations, methodology changes, and observed referral classification
- Core public/onboarding/billing/error journeys, keyboard/screen-reader behavior, accessible forms/charts, and automated accessibility checks
- Generated public HTML/metadata, route inventory, public 404/redirect behavior, app deep-link fallback, public bundle budgets, and refresh of nested prerendered pages
- Public page-map/locale consistency, preview-to-production indexing boundaries, marketing content rollback, and verified feature/offer claims
- Acquisition event deduplication, user-versus-organization counts, payment-confirmed conversion, attribution handoffs, consent gaps, and privacy-safe event properties
- Report-share/recipient revocation, notification deduplication, export safety, and closure/retention enforcement
- Deployment health checks

No generated content quality metric replaces human review. Product usefulness should be measured through accepted recommendations, published content, search changes, conversions, and user feedback.

## 36. Known Risks

- Provider APIs, quotas, model names, and pricing change.
- Social platform access is restrictive and unstable.
- Google Trends API access may remain limited.
- Competitor metrics are often estimates.
- AI can produce unsupported claims without grounding and review.
- Crawlers create SSRF and resource-exhaustion risks.
- A single EC2 instance is a single point of failure.
- PostgreSQL in Docker requires disciplined backup and restore operations.
- Cross-project authorization mistakes would expose sensitive analytics.
- Cost can grow quickly without task-specific models and budgets.
- Trial abuse, concurrent usage races, and delayed billing events can create unbounded spend or incorrect access without authoritative server-side controls.
- Sampled AI answers and identifiable referrals can be overstated as complete visibility without surface, denominator, and attribution labels.
- Generic workflow features can distract from validating the core SEO flow.

These risks are addressed through explicit boundaries, source labels, project scoping, cost records, safe crawling, human approval, backups, and phased delivery.

## 37. Open Decisions For Implementation Time

The following do not need to be decided during the documentation checkpoint:

- Public product domain and final trademark-cleared brand
- Initial SaaS customer segment/positioning, exact public URL/keyword map, approved proof material, and marketing publication/review ownership
- Product launch markets and supported public/app/email/support languages, locale URL conventions, and translation workflow
- Initial framework/runtime pins are selected in D049 within D041's release lines; revisit compatibility and record replacements when upgrading
- Final authenticated UI component library after compatibility review
- Production app and API hostnames
- Exact SES configuration
- First SERP and news providers
- Keyword, backlink, and competitor traffic-estimation providers, licensed history coverage, and budgets
- Exact Bing/Yandex connector capabilities and authentication based on official access documentation at implementation time
- Local profile eligibility/API access, local visibility providers, and optional server-log formats and retention
- Implement D103's customer-entered, scoped BYOK and operator credential configuration before offering those UI flows; verify real provider access separately before advertising it
- D095-D098 select a 30-day no-card trial, first eligible organization automatic and later organizations staff-reviewed, one USD 149 monthly Agency plan with bounded trial/paid allowances, immediate charge/start on early owner checkout, explicit Paddle Billing checkout, monthly auto-renewal with period-end cancellation, API version `1`, USD base/localized TRY presentation, global supported buyers, intended USD wire payout, seven-day failed-payment grace, Paddle/mandatory-law refunds and 90-day lapse/30-day closure/35-day backup-expiry retention. Provider approval, legal/accounting review and P3A-12 cost/readiness validation still gate activation
- First AI-visibility platforms and collection surfaces, prompt/sample budgets, cadence, and provider terms/retention limits
- Customer MFA rollout, final public/app navigation and visual design, and validated support/report delivery preferences
- Exact RPO/RTO targets before production launch
- Public/app URL layout, marketing content authoring/rebuild workflow, and route-specific performance budgets within the accepted Angular static deployment
- Product analytics/consent tooling, funnel event/cohort definitions and attribution windows, and first-party SEO monitoring ownership/cadence

These decisions must be recorded in `docs/DECISIONS.md` when made.

Commercial offer and retention decisions must be resolved before public trial/paid launch; listing them here does not authorize placeholder prices, an arbitrary trial length, or undisclosed automatic charging. Provider documentation and supporting standards are mapped in `.agents/references/saas-product-experience.md` and `.agents/references/seo-data-sources.md`; verify current requirements when implementing each integration.

Launch positioning, page inventory, actual language coverage, content ownership, measurement definitions, and readiness evidence must also be settled before the related public promises go live. These requirements do not select an audience, market, CMS, analytics vendor, or provider on the user's behalf.
